8

We numerically check that they deliver the same estimates even for the small sample sizes
T=25
and
50
.
by restricting its parameter space to
\beta<15
. Without this parameter restriction, the behaviour of the ET estimator is very unstable.
An analysis of the typical shape of the objective functions for small sample size explains this phenomenon. The typical ET objective function has a ridge that follows from around the population parameter values (
\beta_{0}=3
,
\mu_{0}=-.72
) towards
(1000,-600)
. The ridgeline is not totally flat, and it often has a gentle downward slope as we move away from the area near the population parameter values. However, regularly, for some simulated samples, the very top of the ridge is extremely far from the population parameter values, so that ET estimates are very far from the population parameter values. This does not happen for the ESP estimator. The variance term of the ESP objective function ensures that the ridge drops sufficiently as we move away from the maximum that is near the population parameter value. Thus, in line with our finite-sample analysis of the ESP objective (Section
2.2
), the ESP estimator is much more stable.

4.2.
Empirical example

In this section, we present an empirical example from asset pricing. Since
Hansen and Singleton 1982
, moment-based estimation is standard in consumption-based asset pricing. For brevity, we focus on the key features of the example. See
Holcblat and Sowell 2019
for additional information and comparisons.

Table 2.
ET vs. ESP inference (1890–2009)

Empirical moment condition:
\frac{1}{2009-1889}\sum_{t=1890}^{2009}\left[\left(\frac{C_{t}}{C_{t-1}}\right)^{-\theta}(R_{m,t}-R_{f,t})\right]=0
, where

R_{m,t}:=
gross market return,
R_{f,t}:=
risk-free asset gross return,
C_{t}:=
consumption,

and
\theta:=
relative risk aversion;

Normalized ET:=
\exp\negthickspace\left\{T\ln\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau_{T}(.)^{\prime}\psi_{t}(.)}\right]\right\}\negthickspace/\negthickspace\int_{\Theta}\exp\negthickspace\left\{T\ln\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\right]\right\}\mathrm{d}\theta
;

Normalized ESP:=
\hat{f}_{\theta^{*}_{T}}(.)/\negthickspace\int_{\Theta}\hat{f}_{\theta^{*}_{T}}(\theta)\mathrm{d}\theta
;

\hat{\theta}_{\mathrm{ET},T}=\hat{\theta}_{\mathrm{MM},T}=50.3
(bullet) and
\hat{\theta}_{\mathrm{ESP},T}=32.21
(bullet);

ET and ESP support
=[-218.2,289.0]
; 95% ET ALR conf. region=
[18.3,289.0]
(stripe);

95% ESP ALR conf. region=
[15.0,112.7]
(stripe).

(A) ET est. and ALR conf. region.

(B) ESP est. and ALR conf. region.

We estimate the relative risk aversion (RRA)
\theta
of a representative agent of the US economy. Previous studies have shown that existing moment-based estimation approaches often produce unstable RRA parameter estimates. We rely on the following moment condition

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{C_{t}}{C_{t-1}}\right)^{-\theta}(R_{m,t}-R_{f,t})\right]=0,

(11)

where
\frac{C_{t}}{C_{t-1}}
is the growth consumption and
(R_{m,t}-R_{f,t})
the market return in excess of the risk-free rate.
The moment condition, which is common to many consumption-based asset pricing models, and the data are similar to
Julliard and Ghosh 2012
corresponding to standard US data at yearly frequency from Shiller’s website spanning from 1890 to 2009. We report ET and ESP estimates as well as confidence regions based on the inversion of the ALR test statistics of Theorem
2
(p.
2
) with
\check{\theta}_{T}=\theta_{0}
. The latter have the advantage to take into account the whole shape of the objective function unlike
t
-statistics-based confidence regions, which only account
for the shape of the objective function in a neighborhood of the estimate through its standard errors.

In Table
2
, Figures (A) and (B) respectively display the ET term and the ESP approximation. For ease of comparison, the scale is the same, and we normalize both of them so they integrate to one. The normalized ET term is much flatter around its maximum than the normalized ESP approximation. Flatness of the objective function around the estimate has been documented for other existing moment-based estimators, and it has often been regarded as one of the main sources of the instability of the RRA estimates
(
Stock and Wright 2000
;
Neely et al. 2001
, e.g.,)
. Figure (B) shows that the normalized ESP is sharp around the ESP estimator. The relative sharpness of the ESP yields sharper confidence regions : The ESP confidence region is less than half its ET counterpart. In light of the variance penalization term in the ESP objective function (Section
2.2
on p.
2.2
) and the shrinkage-like behavior of the ESP estimator in the Monte-Carlo simulations (Section
4.1
), the relative sharpness of the ESP inference is not surprising. In
Holcblat and Sowell 2019
, additional empirical evidences corroborate the increased stability and precision of the ESP estimator w.r.t. the ET estimator (or equivalently, MM estimator).

5.
Connection to the literature and further research directions

The present paper demonstrates a previously unknown connection between the SP approximation and moment-based estimation, and hence it is related to many papers in addition to the ones already cited.
Following
Daniels 1954
, the literature in
statistics

(
Easton and Ronchetti 1986
;
Spady 1991
;
Jensen 1992
;
Vecchia et al. 2012
;
Broda and Kan 2015
;
Fasiolo et al. 2018
, e.g.,)
and econometrics
(
Phillips 1978
;
Holly and Phillips 1979
;
Phillips 1982
;
Lieberman 1994
;
Aït-Sahalia and Yu 2006
, e.g.,)
has
used the SP (saddlepoint) and ESP approximations to obtain accurate approximations of distributions, especially in the tails. The strand of the SP literature that is closest to our paper derives SP approximations to the distribution of statistics that correspond to solutions of nonlinear estimating equations. The latter strand of literature started with
Field 1982
and continued with
Skovgaard 1990
;
Monti and Ronchetti 1993
;
Imbens 1997
;
Jensen and Wood 1998
;
Almudevar et al. 2000
;
Robinson et al. 2003
, and

Ronchetti and Trojani 2003
, among others.
More recently,
Czellar and Ronchetti 2010
,
Ma and Ronchetti 2011
, and
Lô and Ronchetti 2012
;
Kundhi and Rilstone 2013
;
Kundhi and Rilstone 2015
propose more accurate tests for indirect inference, functional measurement error models, moment condition models, nonlinear estimators and GEL (generalized empirical likelihood) estimators, respectively.
To the best of our knowledge, unlike the present paper, none of the prior papers use the SP or the ESP to develop an estimation method that yields a novel moment-based estimator.
In ongoing work, we generalize the ESP approximation to the over-identified case, and establish further good mathematical properties.

Notes and acknowledgements

Parts of the present paper have previously circulated under the title “The Empirical Saddlepoint Likelihood Estimator Applied to Two-Step GMM”
(
Sowell 2009
)
. Some proofs of the present paper also borrow technical results from
Holcblat 2012
. Helpful comments were provided by Philipp Ketz (discussant), Eric Renault, Aman Ullah and seminar/conference participants at Carnegie Mellon University, CFE-CMStatistics 2017, Swiss Finance Institute (EPFL and the University of Lausanne), 10th French Econometrics Conference (Paris School of Economics), at the Econometric Society European Winter Meeting 2018 (University of Naples Federico II), and at the University of Luxembourg.

ONLINE APPENDIX:

The ESP estimator

Benjamin Holcblat and Fallaw Sowell

This appendix mainly consists of the proofs of Theorem
1
, existence and consistency and asymptotic normality of the ESP estimator, and Theorem
2
, asymptotic distributions of the Trintiy+1 test statistics. The proof of Theorem
1
builds on the traditional uniform convergence proof technique of
Wald 1949
. The proof of Theorem
2
adapts the usual way of deriving the trinity tests.
The length of the proofs is mainly due to the variance term
|\Sigma_{T}(\theta)|_{\det}^{-\frac{1}{2}}
and the high-level of details. The latter should make the proofs more transparent, and should ease the use of the intermediary results in further research.

In addition to the proofs, this appendix contains a table of contents, some formal definitions, the precise assumptions of the paper, a discussion thereof, and additional information regarding the examples.

Appendix A
Definitions and assumptions

Definition 1

(ESP approximation;
Ronchetti and Welsh 1994
)
.

The ESP approximation of the distribution of the solution to the empirical moment conditions (
2
) is

\displaystyle\hat{f}_{\theta^{*}_{T}}(\theta):=\exp\left\{T\ln\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\right]\right\}\left(\frac{T}{2\pi}\right)^{m/2}\left|\Sigma_{T}(\theta)\right|_{\det}^{-\frac{1}{2}}

(12)

where
|.|_{\det}
denotes the determinant function,
\theta^{*}_{T}
a solution to the empirical moment conditions (
2
),
\psi_{t}(.):=\psi(X_{t},.)
, and

\displaystyle\Sigma_{T}(\theta)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\hat{w}_{t,\theta}\frac{\partial\psi_{t}(\theta)}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\right]^{-1}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\hat{w}_{t,\theta}\psi_{t}(\theta)\psi_{t}(\theta)^{\prime}\right]\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\hat{w}_{t,\theta}\frac{\partial\psi_{t}(\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right]^{-1},

(13)

\displaystyle\hat{w}_{t,\theta}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\frac{\exp\left[\tau_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)\right]}{\sum_{i=1}^{T}\exp\left[\tau_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi_{i}(\theta)\right]}\text{ , }

(14)

\displaystyle\tau_{T}(\theta)

such that (s.t.)

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\psi_{t}(\theta)\exp\left[\tau_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)\right]=0_{m\times 1}\text{. }

(15)

Definition 2

(ESP estimator)
.

The ESP estimator
\hat{\theta}_{T}
is a maximizer of the ESP approximation (
12
), i.e.,

\displaystyle\hat{\theta}_{T}\in\arg\max_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\hat{f}_{\theta^{*}_{T}}(\theta).

(16)

We require the following assumption to prove the existence and the consistency of the ESP estimator.

Assumption 1
.

(a)
The data
(X_{t})_{t=1}^{\infty}
are a sequence of i.i.d. random vectors of dimension p on the complete probability sample space
(\mathbf{\Omega},\mathcal{E},\mathbb{P})
.
(b)
Let the moment function
\psi:\mathbf{R}^{p}\times\mathbf{\Theta}^{\epsilon}\mapsto\mathbf{R}^{m}
be s.t.
\theta\mapsto\psi(X_{1},\theta)
is continuously differentiable
\mathbb{P}
-a.s., and
\forall\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}^{\epsilon}
,
x\mapsto\psi(x,\theta)
is
\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{p})/\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{m})
-measurable, where, for
\epsilon>0
,
\mathbf{\Theta}^{\epsilon}
denotes the
\epsilon
-neighborhood of
\mathbf{\Theta}
.
(c)
In the parameter space
\mathbf{\Theta}
, there exists a unique
\theta_{0}\in\mathrm{int}(\mathbf{\Theta})
s.t.
\mathbb{E}\left[\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})\right]=0_{m\times 1}
where
\mathbb{E}
denotes the expectation under
\mathbb{P}
.
(d)
Let the parameter space
\mathbf{\Theta}\subset\mathbf{R}^{m}
be a compact set, s.t., for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}
, there exists
\tau(\theta)\in\mathbf{R}^{m}
that solves the equation
\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi(X_{1},\theta)\right]=0
for
\tau
.
(e)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}^{\epsilon}}\mathrm{e}^{2\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\right]<\infty
where
\mathbf{S}:=\{(\theta,\tau):\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}\&\tau\in\mathbf{T}(\theta)\}
and
\mathbf{T}(\theta):=\overline{B_{\epsilon_{\mathbf{T}}}(\tau(\theta))}
with
\overline{B_{\epsilon_{\mathbf{T}}}(\tau(\theta))}
the closed ball of radius
\epsilon_{\mathbf{T}}>0
and center
\tau(\theta)
.
(f)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}|\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}|^{2}\right]<\infty
, where
|.|
denotes the Euclidean norm.
(g)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}^{\epsilon}}|\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}|^{2}\right]<\infty
.
(h)

For all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}
, the matrices
\left[\mathbb{E}\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\right]
and
\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}\right]
are invertible, so
\Sigma(\theta):=\negthickspace\left[\mathbb{E}\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\right]^{-1}\negthickspace\negthickspace\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}\right]\negthickspace\left[\mathbb{E}\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right]^{-1}
is also invertible.

We require the following additional assumption to prove the asymptotic normality of the ESP estimator.

Assumption 2
.

(a)
The function

\theta\mapsto\psi(X_{1},\theta)
is three times continuously differentiable in a neighborhood
\mathcal{N}
of
\theta_{0}
in
\mathbf{\Theta}

\mathbb{P}
-a.s.
(b)
There exists a
\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{p})/\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R})
-measurable function
b(.)
satisfying
\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\theta\in\mathcal{N}}\sup_{\tau\in\mathbf{T}(\theta)}\mathrm{e}^{k_{1}\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}b(X_{1})^{k_{2}}\right]<\infty
for
k_{1}\in[\![1,2]\!]
and
k_{2}\in[\![1,4]\!]
s.t., for all
j\in[\![0,3]\!]
,
\sup_{\theta\in\mathcal{N}}|\nabla^{j}\psi(X_{1},\theta)|\leqslant b(X_{1})
where
\nabla^{j}\psi(X_{1},\theta)
denotes a vector of all partial derivatives of
\theta\mapsto\psi(X_{1},\theta)
of order
j
.

Assumptions
1
and
2
are stronger than the usual assumptions in the MM literatureè, but are similar to assumptions used
in the entropy literature and related literatures. Assumptions
1
and
2
are essentially adapted from
Haberman 1984
,
Kitamura and Stutzer 1997
, and
Schennach 2007
. See also
Chib et al. 2018
for similar assumptions.
The Appendix
C.1
(p.
C.1
) contains a detailed discussion of Assumptions
1
and
2
.

In addition to Assumptions
1
and
2
, we require the following standard and mild assumption to establish the asymptotic distribution of the Wald, LM, ALR, and ET statistics.

Assumption 3

(For the trinity
+1
)
.

(a)
The function
r:\mathbf{\Theta}\rightarrow\mathbf{R}^{q}
in the null hypothesis (
9
)
is continuously differentiable.
(b)
The derivative
R(\theta):=\frac{\partial r(\theta)}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}
is full rank at
\theta_{0}
.

Appendix B
Proofs

B.1.
Proof of Theorem
1
(i) : Existence and consistency

The proof of Theorem
1
(i) (i.e., consistency) adapts the Wald’s approach to consistency
(
Wald 1949
)
along the lines of
Kitamura and Stutzer 1997
,
Schennach 2007
,
Chib et al. 2018
and others. More precisely, standardizing
the logarithm of the ESP approximation, we show that,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for
T
big enough, the ESP estimator maximizes the LogESP function (
8
) on p.
8
,
where

\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\left|\ln\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\right]-\ln\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]\right|=o(1)
and

\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\left|\frac{1}{2T}\ln|\Sigma_{T}(\theta)|_{\det}\right|=O(T^{-1})
. The two main differences between our proof of Theorem
1
(i) and the proofs available in the entropy literature are the following. Firstly, we need to ensure that, for
T
big enough, for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}
,
|\Sigma_{T}(\theta)|_{\det}
is bounded away from zero, so that the LogESP function (
8
) on p.
8
does not diverge to
\infty
on parts of the parameter space. Secondly, we prove that the joint parameter space for
\theta
and
\tau
(i.e.,
\mathbf{S}
) is a compact set.

Core of the proof of Theorem
1
i.

Under Assumption
1
(a)(b) and (d)-(h), by Lemma
1
(p.
1
),
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for
T
big enough, the ESP approximation and the ESP estimator exist. Moreover, under Assumption
1
(a)-(b) and (d)-(h), by Lemma
6
iv (p.
6
),
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for
T
big enough,
|\Sigma_{T}(\theta)|_{\det}>0
, for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}
. Thus, we can apply the strictly increasing transformation
x\mapsto\frac{1}{T}[\ln(x)-\frac{m}{2}\ln(\frac{T}{2\pi})]
to the ESP approximation in equation (
12
) on p.
12
, so that,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for
T
big enough,

\displaystyle\hat{\theta}_{T}\in\arg\max_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\hat{f}_{\theta^{*}_{T}}(\theta)

(17)

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle\hat{\theta}_{T}\in\arg\max_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\left\{\ln\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\right]-\frac{1}{2T}\ln|\Sigma_{T}(\theta)|_{\det}\right\}.

Now, by the triangle inequality,

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\left|\ln\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\right]-\frac{1}{2T}\ln|\Sigma_{T}(\theta)|_{\det}-\ln\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]\right|

(18)

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\left|\ln\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\right]-\ln\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]\right|+\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\left|\frac{1}{2T}\ln|\Sigma_{T}(\theta)|_{\det}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(1)\text{ $\mathbb{P}$-a.s. as $T\rightarrow\infty$}

where the last equality follow from Lemma
2
iv (p.
2
) and Lemma
6
v (p.
6
) under Assumption
1
(a)-(b) and (d)-(h).
Thus, regarding
\hat{\theta}_{T}
, it is now sufficient to check the assumptions of the standard consistency theorem
(e.g.
Newey and McFadden 1994
, pp. 2121-2122 Theorem 2.1, which is also valid in an almost-sure sense)
. Firstly, under Assumption
1
(a)-(e) and (g)-(h), by Lemma
10
iv (p.
10
),
\theta\mapsto\ln\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]|
is uniquely maximized at
\theta_{0}
, i.e., for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}\setminus\{\theta_{0}\}
,
\ln\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]<\ln\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta_{0})^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})}]=0
. Secondly, under Assumptions
1
(a)(b)(d)(e)(g) and (h), by Lemma
3
(p.
3
),
\theta\mapsto\ln\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]
is continuous in
\mathbf{\Theta}
. Finally, by Assumption
1
(d), the parameter space
\mathbf{\Theta}
is compact.
∎

Lemma 1

(Existence of the ESP approximation and estimator)
.

Under Assumption
1
(a)(b) and (d)-(h),
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for
T
big enough,

(i)

the ESP approximation
\hat{f}_{\theta^{*}_{T}}(.)
exists;

(ii)

\theta\mapsto\tau_{T}(\theta)
is unique and continuously differentiable in
\mathbf{\Theta}
, so that the ESP approximation
\theta\mapsto\hat{f}_{\theta^{*}_{T}}(\theta)
is also unique and continuous in
\mathbf{\Theta}
;

(iii)

for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}
, the ESP approximation
\omega\mapsto\hat{f}_{\theta^{*}_{T}}(\theta)
is
\mathcal{E}/\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R})
-measurable; and

(iv)

there exists an ESP estimator
\hat{\theta}_{T}\in\arg\max_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\hat{f}_{\theta^{*}_{T}}(\theta)
that
is
\mathcal{E}/\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{m})
-measurable.

Proof.

The result follows from Lemmas
2
(p.
2
),
3
(p.
3
) and
6
(p.
6
) and standard arguments. For completeness, a detailed proof is provided.

(i)
Under Assumption
1
(a)(b), (d)-(e)(g) and (h), by Lemma
2
ii (p.
2
),
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. there exists a
\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{\Theta})\otimes\mathcal{E}/\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{m})
-measurable function
\tau_{T}(.)
s.t., for
T
big enough, for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}
,
\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\psi_{t}(\theta)=0_{m\times 1}
and
\tau_{T}(\theta)\in\mathrm{int}[\mathbf{T}(\theta)]
. Moreover, under Assumption
1
(a)(b)(d) (e)(g) and (h), by Lemma
3
(p.
3
) with
\mathrm{P}=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\delta_{X_{t}}
, for all
T\in[\![1,\infty[\![
, for all
(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}
,
0<\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}
, so that, for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}
,
0<\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}
. Thus, the ET term exists. Now, under Assumption
1
(a)-(b) and (d)-(h), by Lemma
6
iv (p.
6
),
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for
T
big enough,
\inf_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}|\Sigma_{T}(\theta)|_{\det}>0
, so that the variance term of the ESP approximation exists. Thus, the ESP approximation exists.

(ii)
By Assumption
1
(b),
\theta\mapsto\psi(X_{1},\theta)
is continuously differentiable in
\mathbf{\Theta}^{\epsilon}

\mathbb{P}
-a.s., so that it is sufficient to show that
\tau_{T}(.)
is unique and continuous, which we prove at once with the standard implicit function theorem. Check its assumptions. Firstly, under Assumption
1
(a)(b), (d)-(e)(g) and (h), by Lemma
2
ii (p.
2
),
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. there exists a function
\tau_{T}(.)
s.t., for
T
big enough, for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}
,
\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\psi_{t}(\theta)=0_{m\times 1}
and
\tau_{T}(\theta)\in\mathrm{int}[\mathbf{T}(\theta)]
. Secondly, for all
\dot{\theta}\in\mathbf{\Theta}
,
\left.\frac{\partial\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\psi_{t}(\theta)\right]}{\partial\tau^{\prime}}\right|_{(\theta,\tau)=(\dot{\theta},\tau_{T}(\dot{\theta}))}=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\dot{\theta})}\psi_{t}(\dot{\theta})\psi_{t}(\dot{\theta})^{\prime}
, which is full rank
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for
T
big enough for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}
, because under Assumption
1
(a)-(b) and (d)-(h), by Lemma
6
iv (p.
6
),
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for
T
big enough,
\inf_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}|\Sigma_{T}(\theta)|_{\det}>0
. Finally, by Assumption
1
(b),
(\theta,\tau)\mapsto\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\psi_{t}(\theta)
is continuously differentiable in
\mathbf{S}^{\epsilon}
.

(iii)
By Assumption
1
(b), for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}
,
x\mapsto\psi(x,\theta)
is
\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{p})/\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{m})
-measurable. Moreover, under Assumption
1
(a)(b), (d)-(e)(g) and (h), by Lemma
2
ii (p.
2
),
\mathbb{P}
-a.s.
\tau_{T}(.)
is a
\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{\Theta})\otimes\mathcal{E}/\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{m})
-measurable function. Thus, the result follows.

(iv)
By Assumption
1
(d),
\mathbf{\Theta}
is compact, so that, by the statements (i)-(iii) of the present lemma, the result follows from the Schmetterer-Jennrich lemma (
Schmetterer 1966
Chap. 5 Lemma 3.3;
Jennrich 1969
Lemma 2).
∎

Lemma 2

(Asymptotic limit of the ET term)
.

Under Assumption
1
(a)(b), (d)-(e)(g) and (h),

(i)

\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.}
as
T\rightarrow\infty
,

\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}-\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]\right|=o(1)
, which implies that
\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.}
as
T\rightarrow\infty
,
\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}}\left|\ln\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\right]-\ln\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]\right|=o(1)\
;

(ii)

\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.}
there exists a
\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{\Theta})\otimes\mathcal{E}/\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{m})
-measurable function
\tau_{T}(.)
s.t., for
T
big enough, for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}
,
\tau_{T}(\theta)\in\arg\min_{\tau\in\mathbf{R}^{m}}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}
,
\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\psi_{t}(\theta)=0_{m\times 1}
and
\tau_{T}(\theta)\in\mathrm{int}[\mathbf{T}(\theta)]
;

(iii)

\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.}
as
T\rightarrow\infty
,
\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\left|\tau_{T}(\theta)-\tau(\theta)\right|=o(1)
;

(iv)

\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.}
as
T\rightarrow\infty
,

\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}-\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]\right|=o(1)
, which implies that
\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.}
as
T\rightarrow\infty
,
\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\left|\ln\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\right]-\ln\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]\right|=o(1).\

Proof.

(i)
Under Assumptions
1
(a)-(b)(d)(e)(g) and (h), by Lemma
4
iii (p.
4
),
\mathbf{S}:=\{(\theta,\tau):\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}\wedge\tau\in\mathbf{T}(\theta)\}
is a compact set.
9
9

9

Note that, unlike what has been sometimes suggested in the entropy literature, if
\mathbf{T}(\theta)
is an unspecified compact set,
\{(\theta,\tau):\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}\wedge\tau\in\mathbf{T}(\theta)\}
does not need to be a compact set :
\{(\theta,\tau):\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}\wedge\tau\in\mathbf{T}(\theta)\}
is not a Cartesian product, but the graph of a correspondence. See Lemma
4
(p.
4
) for more details.
Thus, under Assumption
1
(a)-(b), (d) (e) and (h), the ULLN (uniform law of large numbers) à la Wald
(e.g.,
Ghosh and Ramamoorthi 2003
, pp. 24-25, Theorem 1.3.3)
yields the first part of the result. Now, under Assumptions
1
(a)(b)(d)(e)(g) and (h), by Lemma
3
(p.
3
),
(\theta,\tau)\mapsto\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]
is continuous, so that
\{\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]:(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}\}
is a compact set by Assumption
1
(d) —continuous mappings preserve compactness
(e.g.,
Rudin 1953
, Theorem 4.14)
. Moreover,
x\mapsto\ln x
is continuous, and, under Assumptions
1
(a)(b)(d)(e)(g) and (h), again by Lemma
3
(p.
3
),

0<\inf_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}}\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]
. Thus, we can choose an
\eta\in\left]0,\inf_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}}\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]\right[
s.t.
x\mapsto\ln x
is uniformly continuous on the closed
\eta
-neighborhood of
\{\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]:(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}\}
—continuous mappings on a compact set are uniformly continuous
(e.g.,
Rudin 1953
, Theorem 4.19)
. Then, the second part follows from the first part of the result: By the first part,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. there exists a
\dot{T}\in\mathbf{N}
s.t.,
\forall T\in[\![\dot{T},\infty[\![
,
\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}}|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}-\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]|<\eta/2
.

(ii)-(iii)
The proof follows the overall strategy of
Schennach 2007
. For completeness and in order to justify our different assumptions, we provide a detailed proof. In particular, note that we formally prove that
0<\inf_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\inf_{\tau\in\mathbf{T}(\theta):|\tau-\tau(\theta)|\geqslant\eta}|\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]-\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]|
: See Lemma
5
(p.
5
). Let
\eta\in]0,\epsilon_{\mathbf{T}}]
be a fixed constant. By Assumption
1
(a)(b),
(\theta,\omega)\mapsto\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}
is continuous w.r.t
\theta
and
\mathcal{E}/\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R})
-measurable w.r.t to
\omega
, so that it is
\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{\Theta})\otimes\mathcal{E}/\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R})
-measurable
(e.g.,
Aliprantis and Border 2006/1999
, Lemma 4.51)
. Moreover, under Assumptions
1
(a)-(b)(d)(e)(g) and (h), by Lemma
4
ii (p.
4
),
\theta\mapsto\mathbf{T}(\theta)
is a nonempty compact valued measurable correspondence. Then, by a generalization of the Schmetterer-Jennrich lemma
(e.g.,
Aliprantis and Border 2006/1999
, Theorem 18.19)
, we can define a
\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{\Theta})\otimes\mathcal{E}/\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R})
-measurable function
\tilde{\tau}_{T}(\theta)
s.t., for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}
,
\tilde{\tau}_{T}(\theta)\in\arg\min_{\tau\in\mathbf{T}(\theta)}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}
.
For the present proof, put
\varepsilon:=\inf_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\inf_{\tau\in\mathbf{T}(\theta):|\tau-\tau(\theta)|\geqslant\eta}|\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]-\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]|
, which is strictly positive
10
10

10

The argument requires
\varepsilon>0
. If
\varepsilon=0
, then the upcoming inequality (
19
) is not sufficient to show that
\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}|\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tilde{\tau}_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]-\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]|<\varepsilon
.
by Lemma
5
(p.
5
) under Assumptions
1
(a)(b)(d)(e) and (h).
11
11

11

Strict convexity of
\tau\mapsto\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]
and compactness of
\mathbf{\Theta}
are not sufficient to ensure that
\varepsilon>0
: We also need the continuity of the value function of the first infimum, which we obtain through Berge’s maximum theorem. See Lemma
5
(p.
5
).
Then, by the definition of
\varepsilon
, whenever
\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}|\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tilde{\tau}_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]-\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]|<\varepsilon
, then
\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}|\tilde{\tau}_{T}(\theta)-\tau(\theta)|\leqslant\eta
. We now show that it is happening
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. as
T\rightarrow\infty
. Under Assumptions
1
(a)(b)(d)(e)(g) and (h), by Lemma
10
(p.
10
),
\tau(\theta)=\arg\min_{\tau\in\mathbf{R}^{m}}\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]
, so that

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\left|\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tilde{\tau}_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]-\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]\right|

(19)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\left\{\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tilde{\tau}_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]-\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]\right\}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\left\{\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tilde{\tau}_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]-\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tilde{\tau}_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}+\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tilde{\tau}_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}-\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}-\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]\right\}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leqslant}}

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\left\{\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tilde{\tau}_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]-\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tilde{\tau}_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}+\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}-\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]\right\}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{\leqslant}}

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\left|\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tilde{\tau}_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]-\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tilde{\tau}_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\right|+\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}-\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]\right|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(d)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle o(1)\text{ $\mathbb{P}$-a.s. as $T\rightarrow\infty$.}

(a)
Add and subtract
\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tilde{\tau}_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}
and
\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}
.
(b)
Note that, under Assumption
1
(d) and (e), by definition,
\tau(\theta)\in\mathbf{T}(\theta)
and
\tilde{\tau}_{T}(\theta)\in\arg\min_{\tau\in\mathbf{T}(\theta)}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}
so that
\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tilde{\tau}_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}-\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\leqslant 0
.
(c)
Triangle inequality w.r.t. the uniform norm.
(d)
Under Assumption
1
(d)(e), by definition, for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}
,
\tau(\theta)\in\mathbf{T}(\theta)
and
\tilde{\tau}_{T}(\theta)\in\mathbf{T}(\theta)
so that the conclusion follows from statement (i).

Inequality (
19
) implies that
\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}|\tilde{\tau}_{T}(\theta)-\tau(\theta)|=o(1)

\mathbb{P}
-a.s. as
T\rightarrow\infty
. Moreover, by Assumption
1
(e), for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}
,
\mathbf{T}(\theta)=\overline{B_{\epsilon_{\mathbf{T}}}(\tau(\theta))}
where
\epsilon_{\mathbf{T}}>0
. Thus,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s., for
T
big enough, for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}
,
\tilde{\tau}_{T}(\theta)\in\mathrm{int}[\mathbf{T}(\theta)]
. Now, for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}
,
\tau\mapsto\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}
is a convex function (Lemma
29
i on p.
29
with
\mathrm{P}=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\delta_{X_{t}}
ensures that
\frac{\partial^{2}[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}]}{\partial\tau\partial\tau^{\prime}}=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\psi_{t}(\theta)\psi_{t}(\theta)^{\prime}\geqslant 0
), and the local minimum of a convex function is a global minimum
(e.g.,
Hiriart-Urruty and Lemaréchal 1993/1996
, p. 253)
. Therefore,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for
T
big enough, for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}
,
\tilde{\tau}_{T}(\theta)
minimizes
\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}
not only over
\mathbf{T}(\theta)
, but also over
\mathbf{R}^{m}
, which means that we can put
\tilde{\tau}_{T}(\theta)=\tau_{T}(\theta)
.

(iv)
Addition and subtraction of
\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]
, and the triangle inequality yield
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for
T
big enough

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}-\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}-\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]\right|+\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\left|\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]-\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(1)\text{ , as $T\rightarrow\infty$, }

where the explanations for the last equality are as follows. By the statement (i) of the present lemma,
\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.}
as
T\rightarrow\infty
,

\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}-\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]\right|=o(1)
. Moreover, by the statement (ii) of the present lemma,
\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.}
for
T
big enough,
\tau_{T}(\theta)\in\mathrm{int}[\mathbf{T}(\theta)]
, so that, for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}
,
(\theta,\tau_{T}(\theta))\in\mathbf{S}
. Thus, the first supremum is
o(1)
as
T\rightarrow\infty
. Regarding the second supremum, under Assumption
1
(a)(b)(d)(e)(g) and (h), by Lemma
3
(p.
3
),
(\theta,\tau)\mapsto\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]
is continuous in
\mathbf{S}
. Now, under Assumptions
1
(a)(b)(d)(e)(g) and (h), by Lemma
4
iii (p.
4
),
\mathbf{S}
is compact, so that
(\theta,\tau)\mapsto\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]
is also uniformly continuous in
\mathbf{S}
—continuous functions on compact sets are uniformly continuous
(e.g.,
Rudin 1953
, Theorem 4.19)
. Thus, under Assumption
1
(a)(b), (d)-(e), (g) and (h), by the statement (iii) of the present lemma, which states that
\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\left|\tau_{T}(\theta)-\tau(\theta)\right|=o(1)

\mathbb{P}
-a.s. as
T\rightarrow\infty
, the second supremum is also
o(1)

\mathbb{P}
-a.s. as
T\rightarrow\infty
.

The second part of the result follows from the first part as in the proof of the statement (i) of the present lemma.
∎

Lemma 3
.

Let
\mathrm{P}
be any probability measure, and
\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{P}}
denote the expectation under
\mathrm{P}
. Under Assumption
1
(a)(b)(d)(e)(g) and (h), if
\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{P}}[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]<\infty
, then

0<\inf_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{P}}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]
, so that
0<\inf_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{P}}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]
. Moreover,
(\theta,\tau)\mapsto\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{P}}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]
and
\theta\mapsto\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{P}}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]
are continuous in
\mathbf{S}
and
\mathbf{\Theta}
, respectively. All of these results hold for
\mathrm{P}=\mathbb{P}
under the aforementioned assumptions.

Proof.

Under Assumption
1
(a) and (b), the Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem and the lemma’s assumption
\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{P}}[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]<\infty
imply that
(\theta,\tau)\mapsto\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{P}}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]
is continuous. Moreover, under Assumptions
1
(a)(b)(d)(e)(g) and (h), by Lemma
4
(p.
4
),
\mathbf{S}
is compact, and continuous functions over compact sets reach a minimum
(e.g.,
Rudin 1953
, Theorem 4.16)
. Now, if there exist
(\dot{\tau},\dot{\theta})\in\mathbf{S}
s.t.
0=\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{P}}[\mathrm{e}^{\dot{\tau}^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\dot{\theta})}]
, then
\mathrm{e}^{\dot{\tau}^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\dot{\theta})}=0

\mathrm{P}
-a.s.
(e.g.,
Kallenberg 2002 (1997
, Lemma 1.24)
, which is impossible by definition of the exponential function. Thus,
0<\inf_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{P}}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]
, so that
0<\inf_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{P}}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]
because by the definition of
\mathbf{S}
in Assumption
1
(e), for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}
,
(\theta,\tau(\theta))\in\mathbf{S}
. Regarding the second part of the result, it immediately follows from the Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem, the lemma’s assumption that
\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{P}}[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]<\infty
, and the continuity of
\tau:\mathbf{\Theta}\rightarrow\mathbf{R}^{m}
by Lemma
10
iii (p.
10
) under Assumptions
1
(a)(b)(d)(e)(g) and (h). Regarding the third part of the result, it is sufficient to note that, under Assumption
1
(a)(b), by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,
\mathbb{E}[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]\leqslant\mathbb{E}[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}}\mathrm{e}^{2\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]^{1/2}<\infty
, where the last inequality follows from Assumption
1
(e).
∎

Lemma 4

(Compactness of
\mathbf{S}
)
.

Under Assumptions
1
(a)(b)(d)(e)(g) and (h),

(i)

The closure of the
\epsilon_{\mathbf{T}}
-neighborhood of
\tau(\mathbf{\Theta})
(i.e.,
\overline{\tau(\mathbf{\Theta})^{\epsilon_{\mathbf{T}}}}
) is compact

(ii)

For all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}
, the correspondence
\theta\mapsto\mathbf{T}(\theta)
is nonempty compact-valued and uhc (upper hemi-continuous), and thus measurable;

(iii)

The set
\mathbf{S}:=\{(\theta,\tau):\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}\wedge\tau\in\mathbf{T}(\theta)\}
is compact.

Proof.

(i)
Under Assumptions
1
(a)(b)(d)(e)(g) and (h),
by Lemma
10
iii (p.
10
),
\tau:\mathbf{\Theta}\rightarrow\mathbf{R}^{m}
is continuous. Moreover, by Assumption
1
(d),
\mathbf{\Theta}
is compact. Thus,
\tau(\mathbf{\Theta})
is bounded —continuous mappings preserve compactness
(e.g.,
Rudin 1953
, Theorem 4.14)
. Consequently,
\tau(\mathbf{\Theta})^{\epsilon_{\mathbf{T}}}=:\{\tau\in\mathbf{R}^{m}:\inf_{\tilde{\tau}\in\tau(\mathbf{\Theta})}|\tau-\tilde{\tau}|<\epsilon_{\mathbf{T}}\}
is bounded, which means that its closure
\overline{\tau(\mathbf{\Theta})^{\epsilon_{\mathbf{T}}}}
is closed and bounded, i.e., compact.

(ii)

Proof that
\mathbf{T}
is nonempty and compact valued.
By Assumption
1
(d), for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}
, there exists
\tau(\theta)
s.t.
\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi(X_{1},\theta)]=0
. Thus, for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}
,
\mathbf{T}(\theta)=\overline{B_{\epsilon_{\mathbf{T}}}(\tau(\theta))}
is nonempty. Moreover, by construction,
\overline{B_{\epsilon_{\mathbf{T}}}(\tau(\theta))}
is compact, so that it is nonempty compact valued.

Proof that
\mathbf{T}
is uhc.
Because
\mathbf{T}
is compact valued, we can use the sequential characterization of upper hemicontinuity
(e.g.,
Aliprantis and Border 2006/1999
, Theorem 17.20)
. Let
((\theta_{n},\tau_{n}))_{n\in\mathbf{N}}\in(\mathbf{S})^{\mathbf{N}}
be a sequence s.t., for all
n\in\mathbf{N}
,
\tau_{n}\in\mathbf{T}(\theta_{n})
and
\theta_{n}\rightarrow\bar{\theta}\in\mathbf{\Theta}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. By construction, for all
n\in\mathbf{N}
,
\tau_{n}\in\overline{B_{\epsilon_{\mathbf{T}}}(\tau(\theta_{n}))}\subset\overline{\tau(\mathbf{\Theta})^{\epsilon_{\mathbf{T}}}}
. Moreover, by statement (i),
\overline{\tau(\mathbf{\Theta})^{\epsilon_{\mathbf{T}}}}
is compact, so that there exists a subsequence
(\tau_{\alpha(n)})_{n\in\mathbf{N}}
s.t.
\tau_{\alpha(n)}\rightarrow\bar{\tau}\in\overline{\tau(\mathbf{\Theta})^{\epsilon_{\mathbf{T}}}}
, as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Again, by construction, for all
n\in\mathbf{N}
,
(\theta_{n},\tau_{n})\in\mathbf{S}
, so that
|\tau_{\alpha(n)}-\tau(\theta_{\alpha(n)})|\leqslant\epsilon_{\mathbf{T}}
. Now, under Assumptions
1
(a)(b)(d)(e)(g) and (h), by Lemma
10
iii,

\tau:\mathbf{\Theta}\rightarrow\mathbf{R}^{m}
is continuous. Thus,
|\tau_{\alpha(n)}-\tau(\theta_{\alpha(n)})|\rightarrow|\bar{\tau}-\tau(\bar{\theta})|
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Thus,
|\bar{\tau}-\tau(\bar{\theta})|\leqslant\epsilon_{\mathbf{T}}
, which means that
\bar{\tau}\in\overline{B_{\epsilon_{\mathbf{T}}}(\tau(\bar{\theta}))}=\mathbf{T}(\bar{\theta})
.

Proof that
\mathbf{T}
is measurable.
Let
F
be a closed subset of
\mathbf{R}^{m}
. Then, its complement
F^{c}
is an open subset of
\mathbf{R}^{m}
.
Now, a correspondence is uhc iff the upper inverse image of a open set is an open set
(e.g.,
Aliprantis and Border 2006/1999
, Lemma 17.4)
.
Thus, by the previous paragraph,
\mathbf{T}^{u}(F^{c})\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{\Theta})
, where
\mathbf{T}^{u}
denotes the upper inverse of
\mathbf{T}
. Now, denoting the lower inverse of
\mathbf{T}
with
\mathbf{T}^{l}
, notice that
\mathbf{T}^{u}(F^{c})=[\mathbf{T}^{l}(F)]^{c}

(e.g.,
Aliprantis and Border 2006/1999
, p. 557)
, so that
[\mathbf{T}^{l}(F)]^{c}\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{\Theta})
, which, in turn implies that
\mathbf{T}^{l}(F)\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{\Theta})
because of the stability of
\sigma
-algebras under complementation.

(iii)
Note that the compactness of
\mathbf{\Theta}
and
\mathbf{T}(\theta)
are not sufficient to ensure the compactness of
\mathbf{S}
because
\mathbf{S}
is not a Cartesian product. By the statement (ii) of the present lemma,
\mathbf{T}
is uhc and closed valued, so that it has a closed graph
(e.g.,
Aliprantis and Border 2006/1999
, Theorem 17.10)
, i.e.,
\mathbf{S}
is closed. Now, by construction,
\mathbf{S}
is a subset
[\overline{\tau(\mathbf{\Theta})^{\epsilon_{\mathbf{T}}}}\times\mathbf{\Theta}]
, which is compact by statement (i) and Assumption
1
(d). Thus,
\mathbf{S}
is also compact —in metric spaces, closed subsets of compact sets are compact
(e.g.,
Rudin 1953
, Theorem 2.35)
.
∎

Lemma 5
.

Under Assumptions
1
(a)(b)(d)(e) and (h),

(i)

for any constant
\eta\in]0,\epsilon_{\mathbf{T}}]
, there exists a continuous value function
v:\mathbf{\Theta}\rightarrow\mathbf{R}_{+}
s.t., for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}
,
v(\theta)=\inf_{\tau\in\mathbf{T}(\theta):|\tau-\tau(\theta)|\geqslant\eta}|\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]-\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]|
;

(ii)

for any constant
\eta\in]0,\epsilon_{\mathbf{T}}]
,
0<\inf_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\inf_{\tau\in\mathbf{T}(\theta):|\tau-\tau(\theta)|\geqslant\eta}|\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]-\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]|
.

Proof.

(i)
It is a consequence of Berge’s maximum theorem
(e.g.,
Aliprantis and Border 2006/1999
, Theorem 17.31)
. Thus, it remains to check its assumptions. For the present proof, define the correspondence
\varphi:\mathbf{\Theta}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbf{R}^{m}
s.t.
\varphi(\theta)=\{\tau\in\mathbf{T}(\theta):|\tau-\tau(\theta)|\geqslant\eta\}
, and the function
f:\mathbf{S}\rightarrow\mathbf{R}_{+}
s.t.
f(\theta,\tau)=|\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]-\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]|
.

Proof of the continuity of
f
.
Under Assumption
1
(a)(b)(d)(e)(g) and (h), by Lemma
3
(p.
3
),
(\theta,\tau)\mapsto\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]
and
\theta\mapsto\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]
are continuous in
\mathbf{S}
and
\mathbf{\Theta}
, respectively, so that the continuity of
f
follows immediately.

Proof that
\varphi
is nonempty compact valued.
By the definition of
\mathbf{T}
in Assumption
1
(e), for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}
,
\mathbf{T}(\theta)=\overline{B_{\epsilon_{\mathbf{T}}}(\tau(\theta))}
, so that, for any
\eta\in]0,\epsilon_{\mathbf{T}}]
,
\varphi(\theta)=\overline{B_{\epsilon_{\mathbf{T}}}(\tau(\theta))}\cap\{\tau\in\mathbf{R}^{m}:\eta\leqslant|\tau-\tau(\theta)|\}\neq\emptyset
, i.e.,
\varphi
is nonempty valued. Moreover, for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}
,
\overline{B_{\epsilon_{\mathbf{T}}}(\tau(\theta))}
is a compact set and
\{\tau\in\mathbf{R}^{m}:\eta\leqslant|\tau-\tau(\theta)|\}
is a closed set, so that
\varphi(\theta)
, which is their intersection, is compact
(e.g.,
Rudin 1953
, Theorem 2.35 and the following Corollary)
.

Proof of the upper hemicontinuity of
\varphi
.
Because
\varphi
is compact valued, we can use the sequential characterization of upper hemicontinuity
(e.g.,
Aliprantis and Border 2006/1999
, Theorem 17.20)
. Let
((\theta_{n},\tau_{n}))_{n\in\mathbf{N}}\in\mathbf{S}^{\mathbf{N}}
be a sequence s.t., for all
n\in\mathbf{N}
,
\tau_{n}\in\varphi(\theta_{n})
and
\theta_{n}\rightarrow\bar{\theta}\in\mathbf{\Theta}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Now, under Assumptions
1
(a)(b)(d)(e)(g) and (h), Lemma
4
iii (p.
4
),
\mathbf{S}
is a compact set, so that there exists a subsequence
((\theta_{\alpha(n)},\tau_{\alpha(n)}))_{n\in\mathbf{N}}
s.t.
(\theta_{\alpha(n)},\tau_{\alpha(n)})\rightarrow(\bar{\theta},\bar{\tau})\in\mathbf{S}
, as
n\rightarrow\infty
. The definition of
\mathbf{S}
implies that
\bar{\tau}\in\mathbf{T}(\bar{\theta})
. Thus, it remains to show that
\eta\leqslant|\bar{\tau}-\tau(\bar{\theta})|
in order to conclude that
\bar{\tau}\in\varphi(\bar{\theta})
. By construction, for all
n\in\mathbf{N}
,
\eta\leqslant|\tau_{\alpha(n)}-\tau(\theta_{\alpha(n)})|
. Moreover, under Assumptions
1
(a)(b)(d)(e)(g) and (h), by Lemma
10
iii (p.
10
),

\tau:\mathbf{\Theta}\rightarrow\mathbf{R}^{m}
is continuous, so that
|\tau_{\alpha(n)}-\tau(\theta_{\alpha(n)})|\rightarrow|\bar{\tau}-\tau(\bar{\theta})|
as
n\rightarrow\infty
, which means that
\eta\leqslant|\bar{\tau}-\tau(\bar{\theta})|
.

Proof of the lower hemicontinuity of
\varphi
.
Use the sequential characterization of the lower hemicontinuity
(e.g.,
Aliprantis and Border 2006/1999
, Theorem 17.21)
. Let
(\theta_{n})_{n\in\mathbf{N}}\in\mathbf{\Theta}^{\mathbf{N}}
be a sequence s.t.
\theta_{n}\rightarrow\bar{\theta}\in\mathbf{\Theta}
and
\bar{\tau}\in\varphi(\bar{\theta})
. Define the sequence
(\tau_{n})_{n\in\mathbf{N}}
s.t., for all
n\in\mathbf{N}
,
\tau_{n}=\tau(\theta_{n})+\bar{\tau}-\tau(\bar{\theta})
. By definition of the correspondence
\varphi
, for all
n\in\mathbf{N}
,
|\tau_{n}-\tau(\theta_{n})|=|\bar{\tau}-\tau(\bar{\theta})|\in[\eta,\epsilon_{\mathbf{T}}]
, which implies that
\tau_{n}\in\varphi(\theta_{n})
. Moreover, under Assumptions
1
(a)(b)(d)(e)(g) and (h), by Lemma
10
iii (p.
10
),

\tau:\mathbf{\Theta}\rightarrow\mathbf{R}^{m}
is continuous, so that
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\tau_{n}=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\tau(\theta_{n})+\bar{\tau}-\tau(\bar{\theta})=\tau(\bar{\theta})+\bar{\tau}-\tau(\bar{\theta})=\bar{\tau}
.

(ii)
Under Assumptions
1
(a)(b)(d)(e)(g) and (h), by Lemma
10
(p.
10
), for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}
,
\tau(\theta)
is the unique minimum of the strictly convex minimization problem
\inf_{\tau\in\mathbf{R}^{m}}\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]
. Thus, for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}
,
v(\theta)>0
. Moreover, by Assumption
1
(d),
\mathbf{\Theta}
is compact, and by statement (i) of the present lemma,
v(.)
is continuous. Thus, there exists
\varepsilon_{v}>0
s.t.
\min_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}v(\theta)>\varepsilon_{v}
because a continuous function over a compact set reaches a minimum
(e.g.,
Rudin 1953
, Theorem 4.16)
.
∎

Lemma 6

(Asymptotic limit of the variance term)
.

Under Assumption
1
(a)-(b) and (d)-(h),

(i)

\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for
T
big enough,
0<\inf_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\left|\left|\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\hat{w}_{t,\theta}\frac{\partial\psi_{t}(\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right]\right|_{\det}\right|
;

(ii)

\mathbb{P}
-a.s. as
T\rightarrow\infty
,
\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\left|\Sigma_{T}(\theta)-\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]\Sigma(\theta)\right|=o(1)

(iii)

\theta\mapsto\Sigma(\theta)
and
\theta\mapsto\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]\Sigma(\theta)
are continuous in
\mathbf{\Theta}

(iv)

\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for
T
big enough,
\inf_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}|\Sigma_{T}(\theta)|_{\det}>0
;

(v)

\mathbb{P}
-a.s. as
T\rightarrow\infty
,
\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\left|\ln|\Sigma_{T}(\theta)|_{\det}-\ln\left|\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]\Sigma(\theta)\right|_{\det}\right|=o(1)
, so that, for all
\eta>0
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. as
T\rightarrow\infty
,
\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}|\frac{1}{2T^{\eta}}\ln|\Sigma_{T}(\theta)|_{\det}|=o(1)
.

Proof.

(i)

Under Assumption
1
(a)-(b) and (d)-(h),
by Lemma
7
(p.
7
),
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. as
T\rightarrow\infty
,

\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\left|\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\hat{w}_{t,\theta}\frac{\partial\psi_{t}(\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right]-\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right]\right|=o(1)
, so that it is sufficient to check the invertibility of
\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right]
for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}
and the continuity of
\theta\mapsto\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right]
(Lemma
30
on p.
30
). Firstly, by Assumption
1
(h),
for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}
,
\Sigma(\theta):=\negthickspace\left[\mathbb{E}\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\right]^{-1}\negthickspace\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}\right]\negthickspace\\
\left[\mathbb{E}\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right]^{-1}
is a positive-definite symmetric matrix, and thus
\left[\mathbb{E}\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta}^{\prime}\right]
is invertible. Moreover, under Assumption
1
(a)(b)(d)(e)(g) and (h), by Lemma
3
(p.
3
), and Assumption
1
(e), for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}
,
0<\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]<\infty
, so that
\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right]
is invertible for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}
. Secondly, under Assumption
1
(a)-(b), (e)-(f), by Lemma
8
i (p.
8
),
\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}|\right]<\infty
, so that the Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem and Assumption
1
(b) imply the continuity of
(\theta,\tau)\mapsto\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right]
in
\mathbf{S}
. Moreover, by definition in Assumption
1
(e), for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}
,
(\tau(\theta),\theta)\in\mathbf{S}
, and under Assumption
1
(a)(b)(d)(e)(g) and (h),
by Lemma
10
iii (p.
10
),

\tau:\mathbf{\Theta}\rightarrow\mathbf{R}^{m}
is continuous.
Thus,
\theta\mapsto\left[\mathbb{E}\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta}^{\prime}\right]
is continuous. Then, the continuity of

\theta\mapsto\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right]
follows from Lemma
3
(p.
3
) under Assumption
1
(a)(b)(d)(e)(g) and (h).

(ii)
On one hand, by definition,
\Sigma(\theta)\negthickspace:=\negthickspace\left[\mathbb{E}\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\right]^{-1}\negthickspace\negthickspace\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}\right]\negthickspace\\
\left[\mathbb{E}\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right]^{-1}
, which is symmetric positive definite by Assumption
1
(h), and
\Sigma_{T}(\theta):=\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\hat{w}_{t,\theta}\frac{\partial\psi_{t}(\theta)}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\right]^{-1}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\hat{w}_{t,\theta}\psi_{t}(\theta)\psi_{t}(\theta)^{\prime}\right]\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\hat{w}_{t,\theta}\frac{\partial\psi_{t}(\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right]^{-1}
, which is well defined
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for
T
big enough by the statement (i) of the present lemma. On the other hand,
under Assumption
1
(a)-(b) and (d)-(h),
by Lemma
7
iii (p.
7
),
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. as
T\rightarrow\infty
,

\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}|\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\hat{w}_{t,\theta}\frac{\partial\psi_{t}(\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right]-\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right]|=o(1)
, and, under Assumptions
1
(a)-(b), (d)-(e) and (g)-(h), by Lemma
8
(p.
8
),
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. as
T\rightarrow\infty
,
\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}|\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\psi_{t}(\theta)\psi_{t}(\theta)^{\prime}\right]-\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}\right]|=o(1)
. Thus, the claim follows from the continuity of the inverse transformation
(e.g.,
Rudin 1953
, Theorem 9.8)
and the limiting functions, and the compactness of
\mathbf{\Theta}
.

(iii)
Under Assumption
1
(a)-(b), (e)-(g), by Lemma
7
i (p.
7
) and
8
(p.
8
),

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}|\right]<\infty
and
\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}|\right]<\infty
, so that, by the Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem and Assumption
1
(b),

(\theta,\tau)\mapsto\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right]
and
(\theta,\tau)\mapsto\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}\right]

are continuous in
\mathbf{S}
. Moreover, by definition in Assumption
1
(e), for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}
,
(\theta,\tau(\theta))\in\mathbf{S}
, and under Assumption
1
(a)(b)(d)(e)(g) and (h),
by Lemma
10
iii (p.
10
),

\tau:\mathbf{\Theta}\rightarrow\mathbf{R}^{m}
is continuous.
Thus
\theta\mapsto\Sigma(\theta)
is continuous, which is the first result. Under Assumption
1
(a)(b)(d)(e)(g) and (h), the second result follows from Lemma
3
(p.
3
), which states that
\theta\mapsto\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]
is also continuous.

(iv)
By construction,
\Sigma_{T}(\theta)
is a symmetric positive semi-definite matrix (Lemma
29
i on p.
29
with
\mathrm{P}=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\delta_{X_{t}}
), so that
|\Sigma_{T}(\theta)|_{\det}\geqslant 0
.
Thus, by the statement (ii) and (iii) of present lemma, it is sufficient to check the invertibility of
\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]\Sigma(\theta)
for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}
(Lemma
30
on p.
30
). By Assumption
1
(h),
for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}
,

\Sigma(\theta):=\negthickspace\left[\mathbb{E}\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\right]^{-1}\negthickspace\negthickspace\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}\right]\left[\mathbb{E}\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right]^{-1}

is a positive-definite symmetric matrix, and thus a fortiori invertible. Moreover, under Assumption
1
(a)(b)(d)(e)(g) and (h), by Lemma
3
(p.
3
), and Assumption
1
(e), for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}
,
0<\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]<\infty
, so that it is also invertible.

(v)
Under Assumption
1
(a)(b)(d)(e)(g) and (h), by Lemma
3
(p.
3
) with
\mathrm{P}=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\delta_{X_{t}}
and by the statement (iv) of the present lemma,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for
T
big enough,
\ln|\Sigma_{T}(\theta)|_{\det}
is well-defined in
\mathbf{\Theta}
. Similarly, under Assumption
1
(a)(b)(d)(e)(g) and (h), by Lemma
3
(p.
3
) and Assumption
1
(h),
\ln\left|\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]\Sigma(\theta)\right|_{\det}
is well-defined in
\mathbf{\Theta}
. Then, the first part of the result follows from the statement (ii) of the present lemma. Regarding the second part, by the triangle inequality,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. as
T\rightarrow\infty
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T^{\eta}}\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\left|\ln|\Sigma_{T}(\theta)|_{\det}\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T^{\eta}}\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\left|\ln\left[|\Sigma_{T}(\theta)|_{\det}\right]-\ln\left[\left|\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]\Sigma(\theta)\right|_{\det}\right]\right|+\frac{1}{T^{\eta}}\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\left|\ln\left[\left|\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]\Sigma(\theta)\right|_{\det}\right]\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(1)

where the explanations of the last equality are as follows. Under Assumption
1
(a)-(b) and (d)-(h), by the statement (iii) of the present lemma
\theta\mapsto\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]\Sigma(\theta)
is continuous in
\mathbf{\Theta}
, which is a compact set by Assumption
1
(d). Now, continuous functions over compact sets are bounded
(e.g.,
Rudin 1953
, Theorem 4.16)
, so that
\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\left|\ln\left[\left|\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]\Sigma(\theta)\right|_{\det}\right]\right|
is bounded, which, in turn, implies that
\frac{1}{T^{\eta}}\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\left|\ln\left[\left|\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]\Sigma(\theta)\right|_{\det}\right]\right|=o(1)
, as
T\rightarrow\infty
. Now the last equality follows from the statement (iv) of the present lemma.∎

Lemma 7
.

Under Assumptions
1
(a)-(b) and (e)-(f),

(i)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}|\right]<\infty
;

(ii)

under additional Assumption
1
(d)(g) and (h),
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. as
T\rightarrow\infty
,

\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}}\left|\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi_{t}(\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right]\right|=o(1)
, so that

\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\left|\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi_{t}(\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right]\right|=o(1)
; and

(iii)

under additional Assumption
1
(d)(g) and (h),
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. as
T\rightarrow\infty
,

\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\left|\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\hat{w}_{t,\theta}\frac{\partial\psi_{t}(\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right]-\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right]\right|=o(1)

Proof.

(i)
The supremum of the absolute value of the product is smaller than the product of the suprema of the absolute values. Thus,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}|\right]

(20)

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}|\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}}\left|\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right|\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leqslant}}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}|^{2}\right]^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\left|\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right|^{2}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{<}}

\displaystyle\infty

(a)
Firstly, note that the expression in the second supremum does not depend on
\tau
, so that
\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}}\left|\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right|=\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\left|\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right|
. Secondly apply the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality. Finally, note that
[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}|]^{2}=\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}|^{2}
and
[\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\left|\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right|]^{2}=\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\left|\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right|^{2}
because
x\mapsto x^{2}
is increasing on
\mathbf{R}_{+}
.

(b)
Note that
|\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}|^{2}=\mathrm{e}^{2\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}
, and then apply Assumption
1
(e) to the first term. Then, application of Assumption
1
(f) to the second term yields the result.

(ii)
By the triangle inequality, as
T\rightarrow\infty

\mathbb{P}
-a.s.,

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\left|\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi_{t}(\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\left|\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi_{t}(\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right]\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(1)

where the explanations for the last equality are as follows. Regarding the first supremum, under Assumptions
1
(a)-(b)(d)(e)(g) and (h), by Lemma
4
iii (p.
4
),
\mathbf{S}:=\{(\theta,\tau):\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}\wedge\tau\in\mathbf{T}(\theta)\}
is a compact set, so that Assumptions
1
(a)-(b), the statement (i) of the present lemma and the ULLN (uniform law of large numbers) à la Wald
(e.g.,
Ghosh and Ramamoorthi 2003
, pp. 24-25, Theorem 1.3.3)
imply that,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. as
T\rightarrow\infty
,

\displaystyle\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}}\left|\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi_{t}(\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right]\right|=o(1).

Now, by Assumption
1
(e), for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}
,
\tau(\theta)\in\mathbf{T}(\theta)
, and under Assumption
1
(a)(b), (d)-(e), (g) and (h), by Lemma
2
ii (p.
2
),
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for
T
big enough, for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}
,
\tau_{T}(\theta)\in\mathbf{T}(\theta)
. Moreover, under Assumption
1
(a)(b), (d)-(e), (g) and (h), by Lemma
2
iii (p.
2
),
\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\left|\tau_{T}(\theta)-\tau(\theta)\right|=o(1)

\mathbb{P}
-a.s. as
T\rightarrow\infty
. Thus, the first supremum is
o(1)
, i.e.,
\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi_{t}(\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}-\mathbb{E}\mathrm{e}^{\tau_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}|=o(1)
, as
T\rightarrow\infty

\mathbb{P}
-a.s. Regarding the second supremum, by Assumption
1
(b),
(\theta,\tau)\mapsto\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}
is continuous. Moreover under Assumptions
1
(a)-(b), and (e)-(f), by the statement (i) of the present lemma,
\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}|\right]<\infty
. Thus, by the Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem and Assumption
1
(b),
(\theta,\tau)\mapsto\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right]
is also continuous in
\mathbf{S}
. Now, under Assumptions
1
(a)(b)(d)(e)(g) and (h), by Lemma
4
iii (p.
4
),
\mathbf{S}
is compact, so that
(\theta,\tau)\mapsto\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right]
is uniformly continuous in
\mathbf{S}
—continuous functions on compact sets are uniformly continuous
(e.g.,
Rudin 1953
, Theorem 4.19)
. Thus, under Assumption
1
(a)(b), (d)-(e), (g) and (h), by Lemma
2
iii (p.
2
), which states that
\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\left|\tau_{T}(\theta)-\tau(\theta)\right|=o(1)

\mathbb{P}
-a.s. as
T\rightarrow\infty
, the second supremum is also
o(1)

\mathbb{P}
-a.s. as
T\rightarrow\infty
.

(iii)
Under Assumptions
1
(a)(b)(d)(e)(g) and (h), Lemma
3
(p.
3
) yields

0<\inf_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}
with
\mathrm{P}=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\delta_{X_{t}}
, and

0<\inf_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}}\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]
with
\mathrm{P}=\mathbb{P}
. Consequently, under Assumption
1
(a)(b), (d)-(f), (g) and (h), by Lemma
2
iii and iv (p.
2
) and the statement (ii) of the present lemma, as
T\rightarrow\infty
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s., uniformly w.r.t.
\theta

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\hat{w}_{t,\theta}\frac{\partial\psi_{t}(\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\frac{1}{T}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi_{i}(\theta)}}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{}{}\mathrm{e}^{\tau_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi_{t}(\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta}^{\prime}\right].

∎

Lemma 8
.

Under Assumptions
1
(a)-(b), (e) and (g),

(i)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}^{\epsilon}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}|\right]<\infty

(ii)

under additional Assumption
1
(d) and (h),
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. as
T\rightarrow\infty
,

\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\psi_{t}(\theta)\psi_{t}(\theta)^{\prime}-\mathbb{E}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}\right|=o(1)
, so that

\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\psi_{t}(\theta)\psi_{t}(\theta)^{\prime}-\mathbb{E}\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}\right|=o(1)

(iii)

under additional Assumption
1
(d)(f) and (h),
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. as
T\rightarrow\infty
,

\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\left|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\hat{w}_{t,\theta}\psi_{t}(\theta)\psi_{t}(\theta)^{\prime}-\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]}\mathbb{E}\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}\right|=o(1)
.

Proof.

The proof is the same as for Lemma
7
with
\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}
and
\psi_{t}(\theta)\psi_{t}(\theta)^{\prime}
in lieu of
\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}
and
\frac{\partial\psi_{t}(\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}
, respectively. For completeness, we provide a proof.

(i)
The supremum of the absolute value of the product is smaller than the product of the suprema of the absolute values. Thus,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}^{\epsilon}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}|\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}^{\epsilon}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}|\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}^{\epsilon}}\left|\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}\right|\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leqslant}}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}^{\epsilon}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}|^{2}\right]^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}^{\epsilon}}\left|\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}\right|^{2}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{<}}

\displaystyle\infty.

(a)
Firstly, for any
(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}^{\epsilon}
,
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}^{\epsilon}
because, for all
(\tilde{\tau},\tilde{\theta})\in\mathbf{S}
,
|\theta-\tilde{\theta}|=\sqrt{\sum_{k=1}^{m}(\theta_{k}-\tilde{\theta}_{k})^{2}}\leqslant\sqrt{\sum_{k=1}^{m}(\theta_{k}-\tilde{\theta}_{k})^{2}+\sum_{k=1}^{m}(\tau_{k}-\tilde{\tau}_{k})^{2}}=|(\theta,\tau)-(\tilde{\tau},\tilde{\theta})|<\epsilon
. Thus, as the second supremum does not depend on
\tau
,
\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}^{\epsilon}}\left|\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}\right|\leqslant\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}^{\epsilon}}\left|\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}\right|
. Secondly apply the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality. Finally,
[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}^{\epsilon}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}|]^{2}=\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}^{\epsilon}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}|^{2}
and
[\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}^{\epsilon}}\left|\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}\right|]^{2}=\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}^{\epsilon}}\left|\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}\right|^{2}
because
x\mapsto x^{2}
is increasing on
\mathbf{R}_{+}
.

(b)
Note that
|\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}|^{2}=\mathrm{e}^{2\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}
, and then apply Assumption
1
(e) to the first term. Then, application of Assumption
1
(g) to the second term yields the result.

(ii)
By the triangle inequality,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. as
T\rightarrow\infty
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\left|\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\psi_{t}(\theta)\psi_{t}(\theta)^{\prime}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\left|\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\psi_{t}(\theta)\psi_{t}(\theta)^{\prime}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}\right]\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(1)

where the explanations for the last equality are as follows. Regarding the first supremum, under Assumptions
1
(a)-(b)(d)(e)(g) and (h), by Lemma
4
iii (p.
4
),
\mathbf{S}:=\{(\theta,\tau):\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}\wedge\tau\in\mathbf{T}(\theta)\}
is a compact set, so that Assumption
1
(a)-(b), the statement (i) of the present lemma and the ULLN (uniform law of large numbers) à la Wald yields that
(e.g.,
Ghosh and Ramamoorthi 2003
, pp. 24-25, Theorem 1.3.3)
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. as
T\rightarrow\infty
,

\displaystyle\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}}\left|\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\psi_{t}(\theta)\psi_{t}(\theta)^{\prime}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}\right]\right|=o(1).

Now, by Assumption
1
(e), for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}
,
\tau(\theta)\in\mathbf{T}(\theta)
, and under Assumption
1
(a)(b), (d)-(e), (g) and (h), by Lemma

ii (p.
2
),
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for
T
big enough, for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}
,
\tau_{T}(\theta)\in\mathbf{T}(\theta)
. Moreover, under Assumption
1
(a)(b), (d)-(e), (g) and (h), by Lemma
2
iii (p.

),
\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\left|\tau_{T}(\theta)-\tau(\theta)\right|=o(1)

\mathbb{P}
-a.s. as
T\rightarrow\infty
. Thus, the first supremum is
o(1)
, i.e.,
\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\psi_{t}(\theta)\psi_{t}(\theta)^{\prime}-\mathbb{E}\mathrm{e}^{\tau_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}|=o(1)
, as

\mathbb{P}
-a.s. Regarding the second supremum, by Assumption

(\theta,\tau)\mapsto\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}
is continuous in
\mathbf{S}
. Moreover under Assumptions
1
(a)-(b), (e) and (g), by the statement (i) of the present lemma,
\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}|\right]<\infty
. Thus, by the Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem and Assumption
1

(\theta,\tau)\mapsto\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}\right]
is also continuous. Now, under Assumptions
1
(a)(b)(d)(e)(g) and (h), by Lemma
4
iii (p.
4
),
\mathbf{S}
is compact, so that
(\theta,\tau)\mapsto\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}\right]
is uniformly continuous —continuous functions on compact sets are uniformly continuous
(e.g.,
Rudin 1953
, Theorem 4.19)
. Thus, under Assumption
1
(a)(b), (d)-(e), (g) and (h), by Lemma
2

2
), which states that
\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}}\left|\tau_{T}(\theta)-\tau(\theta)\right|=o(1)

\mathbb{P}
-a.s. as

, the second supremum is also
o(1)

\mathbb{P}
-a.s. as
T\rightarrow\infty
.

(iii)
Under Assumptions
1

(p.

) yields

with
\mathrm{P}=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\delta_{X_{t}}
, and

. Consequently, under Assumption

) and the statement (ii) of the present lemma, as

,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s., uniformly w.r.t.

,

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\hat{w}_{t,\theta}\psi_{t}(\theta)\psi_{t}(\theta)^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\frac{1}{T}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi_{i}(\theta)}}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{}{}\mathrm{e}^{\tau_{T}(\theta)^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\psi_{t}(\theta)\psi_{t}(\theta)^{\prime}

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}\right].
