Lemma 9
.

Under Assumptions
1
(a)(b)(g),

(i)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}^{\epsilon}}\left|\psi(X_{1},\theta)\right|^{4}\right]<\infty
, so that
\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}^{\epsilon}}\left|\psi(X_{1},\theta)\right|^{2}\right]<\infty
; and

(ii)

under additional Assumption
1
(e),
\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}^{\epsilon}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi(X_{1},\theta)|\right]<\infty

Proof.

(i)
Put
\psi(X_{1},\theta)=:(\psi_{1}(X_{1},\theta)\;\psi_{2}(X_{1},\theta)\;\cdots\;\psi_{m}(X_{1},\theta))^{\prime}
. Note that
\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}^{\epsilon}}\left|\psi(X_{1},\theta)\right|^{4}=[\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}^{\epsilon}}\left|\psi(X_{1},\theta)|^{2}\right]^{2}
because
x\mapsto x^{2}
is an increasing function. Thus, by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,
\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}^{\epsilon}}\left|\psi(X_{1},\theta)\right|^{2}\right]\leqslant\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left\{\left[\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}^{\epsilon}}\left|\psi(X_{1},\theta)\right|^{2}\right]^{2}\right\}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{=}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left\{\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}^{\epsilon}}\left|\psi(X_{1},\theta)\right|^{4}\right\}}
, so that it remains to show the first part of the statement. On one hand,
by the definition of the Euclidean norm,

\displaystyle\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left\{\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}^{\epsilon}}\left|\psi(X_{1},\theta)\right|^{4}\right\}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left\{\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}^{\epsilon}}\left[\left(\sum_{k=1}^{m}\psi_{k}(X_{1},\theta)^{2}\right)^{2}\right]\right\}}

(21)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\leqslant}}

\displaystyle\sqrt{m\mathbb{E}\left\{\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}^{\epsilon}}\left[\sum_{k=1}^{m}\psi_{k}(X_{1},\theta)^{4}\right]\right\}}

where the explanation for the last inequality is as follows. By the Jensen’s inequality,

\left(\frac{1}{m}\sum_{k=1}^{m}a_{k}\right)^{2}\leqslant\frac{1}{m}\sum_{k=1}^{m}a_{k}^{2}
, so that
\left(\sum_{k=1}^{m}a_{k}\right)^{2}\leqslant m\sum_{k=1}^{m}a_{k}^{2}
. Apply the later inequality with
\psi_{k}(X_{1},\theta)^{2}=a_{k}
.

On the other hand,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}^{\epsilon}}\left|\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}\right|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}^{\epsilon}}\left|\begin{pmatrix}\psi_{1}(X_{1},\theta)^{2}&\psi_{1}(X_{1},\theta)\psi_{2}(X_{1},\theta)&\cdots&\psi_{1}(X_{1},\theta)\psi_{m}(X_{1},\theta)\\
\psi_{2}(X_{1},\theta)\psi_{1}(X_{1},\theta)&\psi_{2}(X_{1},\theta)^{2}&\cdots&\psi_{2}(X_{1},\theta)\psi_{m}(X_{1},\theta)\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\psi_{m}(X_{1},\theta)\psi_{1}(X_{1},\theta)&\psi_{m}(X_{1},\theta)\psi_{2}(X_{1},\theta)&\cdots&\psi_{m}(X_{1},\theta)^{2}\\
\end{pmatrix}\right|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\{\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}^{\epsilon}}\left[\sum_{(i,j)\in[\![1,m]\!]^{2}}\left[\psi_{i}(X_{1},\theta)\psi_{j}(X_{1},\theta)\right]^{2}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\{\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}^{\epsilon}}\left[\sum_{k=1}^{m}\psi_{k}(X_{1},\theta)^{4}+\sum_{(i,j)\in[\![1,m]\!]^{2}:i\neq j}\left[\psi_{i}(X_{1},\theta)\psi_{j}(X_{1},\theta)\right]^{2}\right]\right\}

Therefore,
\sum_{k=1}^{m}\psi_{k}(X_{1},\theta)^{4}\leqslant\sum_{k=1}^{m}\psi_{k}(X_{1},\theta)^{4}+\sum_{(i,j)\in[\![1,m]\!]^{2}:i\neq j}\left[\psi_{i}(X_{1},\theta)\psi_{j}(X_{1},\theta)\right]^{2}
,

the later equality and inequality (
21
) yield

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}^{\epsilon}}\left|\psi(X_{1},\theta)\right|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\sqrt{m\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}^{\epsilon}}\left|\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}\right|^{2}\right]}

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\infty

where the last inequality follows from Assumption
1
(g).

(ii)
The supremum of the absolute value of the product is smaller than the product of the suprema of the absolute values. Thus,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}^{\epsilon}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi(X_{1},\theta)|\right]

(22)

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}^{\epsilon}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}|\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}^{\epsilon}}\left|\psi(X_{1},\theta)\right|\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leqslant}}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}^{\epsilon}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}|^{2}\right]^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}^{\epsilon}}|\psi(X_{1},\theta)|^{2}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{<}}

\displaystyle\infty

(a)
Firstly, for any
(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}^{\epsilon}
,
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}^{\epsilon}
because, for all
(\tilde{\tau},\tilde{\theta})\in\mathbf{S}
,
|\theta-\tilde{\theta}|=\sqrt{\sum_{k=1}^{m}(\theta_{k}-\tilde{\theta}_{k})^{2}}\leqslant\sqrt{\sum_{k=1}^{m}(\theta_{k}-\tilde{\theta}_{k})^{2}+\sum_{k=1}^{m}(\tau_{k}-\tilde{\tau}_{k})^{2}}=|(\theta,\tau)-(\tilde{\tau},\tilde{\theta})|<\epsilon
. Thus, as the expression in the second supremum does not depend on
\tau
,
\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}^{\epsilon}}\left|\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}\right|\leqslant\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}^{\epsilon}}\left|\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}\right|
. Secondly apply the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality. Finally, note that
[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}^{\epsilon}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}|]^{2}=\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}^{\epsilon}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}|^{2}
and
[\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}^{\epsilon}}\left|\psi(X_{1},\theta)\right|]^{2}=\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}^{\epsilon}}\left|\psi(X_{1},\theta)\right|^{2}
because
x\mapsto x^{2}
is increasing on
\mathbf{R}_{+}
.

(b)
Note that
|\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}|^{2}=\mathrm{e}^{2\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}
, and then apply Assumption
1
(e) to the first term. Then, application of the statement (i) of the present lemma to the second term yields the result.
∎

Remark 1
.

The first step of the proof shows that even the fourth moment is uniformly bounded.
\diamond

Lemma 10

(Implicit function
\tau(.)
)
.

Under Assumption
1
(a)(b)(e)(g) and (h),

(i)

for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}
,
\tau\mapsto\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\right]
is a strictly convex function s.t.
\frac{\partial\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\right]}{\partial\tau}=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi(X_{1},\theta)\right]
;

(ii)

under additional Assumption
1
(d), for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}
, there exists a unique
\tau(\theta)
such that
\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi(X_{1},\theta)\right]=0
; and

(iii)

under additional Assumption
1
(d),

\tau:\mathbf{\Theta}\rightarrow\mathbf{R}^{m}
is continuous; and

(iv)

under additional Assumption
1
(c) and (d), for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}\setminus\{\theta_{0}\}
,
\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\right]<\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta_{0})^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})}\right]=1
where
\tau(\theta_{0})=0_{m\times 1}
.

Proof.

(i)
Under Assumption
1
(a) and (b), by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}^{\epsilon}}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\right]\leqslant\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}^{\epsilon}}\mathrm{e}^{2\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\right]^{1/2}
, which is finite by Assumption
1
(e). Now, by Assumption
1
(e), for all
\dot{\theta}\in\mathbf{\Theta}
,
\tau(\dot{\theta})\in\mathrm{int}[\mathbf{T}(\dot{\theta})]
. Then, by a standard result on Laplace’s transform
(e.g.,
Monfort (1980
, Theorems 3 on p. 183)
,
\tau\mapsto\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\dot{\theta})}\right]
is
C^{\infty}
in a neighborhood of
\tau(\dot{\theta})
, and
\tau\mapsto\frac{\partial\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\dot{\theta})}\right]}{\partial\tau}=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\dot{\theta})}\psi(X_{1},\theta)\right]
and
\tau\mapsto\frac{\partial^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\dot{\theta})}\right]}{\partial\tau\partial\tau^{\prime}}=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\dot{\theta})}\psi(X_{1},\dot{\theta})\psi(X_{1},\dot{\theta})^{\prime}\right]
. Moreover, under Assumptions
1
(a)-(b), (e) and (g), Assumption
1
(h) implies that,

\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\dot{\theta})}\psi(X_{1},\dot{\theta})\psi(X_{1},\dot{\theta})^{\prime}\right]
is a symmetric positive-definite matrix because a well-defined covariance matrix is invertible iff it is invertible under an equivalent probability measure (Lemma
29
and Corollary
1
i on p.
1
).

(ii)
Assumption
1
(d) ensures existence, while the statement (i) of the present lemma ensures that
\tau(\theta)
is the solution of a strictly convex problem, so that it is unique.

(iii)
Note that, under our assumptions, an application of the standard implicit function
(e.g.,
Rudin 1953
, Theorem 9.28)
is not directly possible as it requires
(\theta,\tau)\mapsto\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi(X_{1},\theta)\right]
to be continuously differentiable in
\mathbf{S}^{\epsilon}
, which, in turn, typically requires to uniformly bound the derivative of the latter in
\mathbf{S}^{\epsilon}

(e.g.,
Davidson 1994
, Theorem 9.31)
.
Thus, we apply the sufficiency part of Kumagai’s implicit function theorem
(
Kumagai 1980
)
. Check its assumptions. Firstly, under Assumptions
1
(a)(b)(e) and (g), by Lemma
9
ii (p.
9
) and the Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem,
(\theta,\tau)\mapsto\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi(X_{1},\theta)\right]
is continuous in
\mathbf{S}^{\epsilon}
, i.e., in an open neighborhood of every
(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}
. Secondly, by the inverse function theorem applied to
\tau\mapsto\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi(X_{1},\theta)\right]

(e.g.,
Rudin 1953
, Theorem 9.24)
, for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}^{\epsilon}
,
\tau\mapsto\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi(X_{1},\theta)\right]
is locally one-to-one :
12
12

12

Here it is necessary to work in an
\epsilon
-neighborhood of
\mathbf{\Theta}
in order to satisfy the assumption of Kumagai’s implicit function theorem
(
Kumagai 1980
)
. The standard implicit function theorem would also require the existence of open neighborhoods around the parameter values at which the function is zero.
As explained in the proof of (i),
under Assumption
1
(a)(b)(e) and (h),
\tau\mapsto\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi(X_{1},\theta)\right]
is continuously differentiable and, under Assumption
1
(a)(b)(e)(g) and (h), for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}
,
\frac{\partial\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi(X_{1},\theta)\right]}{\partial\tau^{\prime}}=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}\right]
is invertible, so that the assumptions of the inverse function theorem are valid.

(iv)
By the statements (i) and (ii) of the present lemma, for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}
, for all
\tau\neq\tau(\theta)
,
\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]<\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]
. Now, for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}\setminus\{\theta_{0}\}
,
\tau(\theta)\neq 0_{m\times 1}
: If there existed
\dot{\theta}\in\mathbf{\Theta}\setminus\{\theta_{0}\}
s.t.
\tau(\dot{\theta})=0_{m\times 1}
, then
0=\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\dot{\theta})^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\dot{\theta})}\psi(X_{1},\dot{\theta})]=\mathbb{E}[\psi(X_{1},\dot{\theta})]
, which would contradict Assumption
1
(c). Thus, for all
\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}\setminus\{\theta_{0}\}
,
\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta)^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]<\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{0_{1\times m}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]=1
. Then, the result follows by the statement (ii) of the present lemma because
0_{m\times 1}=\mathbb{E}[\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})]=\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{0_{1\times m}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})]
.
∎

B.2.
Decomposition and derivatives of the log-ESP
L_{T}(.,.)

In this section, we simplify
L_{T}(\theta,\tau)
and study its derivatives. Such results are needed for the proof of Theorem
1
ii and other results afterwards.

Lemma 11
.

Under Assumption
1
(a)-(e) and (g)(h), by Lemma
10
(p.
10
), define
\tau(\theta_{0})=\tau_{0}=0_{m\times 1}
. Under Assumption
1
(a)-(b), (e) and (h),

(i)

under additional Assumption
1
(d) and (g), there exist
(\underline{M}_{\mathrm{e}},\overline{M}_{\mathrm{e}})\in\mathbf{R}_{+}\setminus\{0\}
s.t.
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for
T
big enough,
\underline{M}_{\mathrm{e}}<\inf_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}
and
\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}<\overline{M}_{\mathrm{e}}
;

(ii)

under additional Assumption
1
(c)(d) and (g), there exists an open ball
B_{r}(\theta_{0},\tau_{0})
centered at
(\theta_{0},\tau_{0})
of radius
r>0
, which is a subset of
\mathbf{S}
;

(iii)

under additional Assumption
1
(c)(d)(f) and (g), for all
(\theta,\tau)
in a closed ball
\overline{B_{r_{\partial}}(\theta_{0},\tau_{0})}\subset\mathbf{S}
centered at
(\theta_{0},\tau_{0})
with radius
r_{\partial}>0
,
|\mathbb{E}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}|_{\det}^{2}>0
, so that,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for
T
big enough,
|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi_{t}(\theta)}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}|_{\det}^{2}>0
;

(iv)

under additional Assumption
1
(g),
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for
T
big enough,

\inf_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}}\negthickspace|\frac{1}{T}\negthickspace\sum_{t=1}^{T}\negthickspace\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\psi_{t}(\theta)\psi_{t}(\theta)^{\prime}|_{\det}>0
.

Proof.

(i)
Under Assumption
1
(a)(b)(d)(e)(g) and (h), by Lemma
2
i (p.
2
), which states that,
\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.}
as
T\rightarrow\infty
,
\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}-\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]\right|=o(1)
, and Lemma
3
(p.
3
) with
\mathrm{P}=\mathbb{P}
, which states that
0<\inf_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}}\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}]
, the result follows.

(ii)
First of all, note that the result is not completely immediate, as
\mathbf{S}:=\{(\theta,\tau):\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}\wedge\tau\in\overline{B_{\epsilon_{\mathbf{T}}}(\tau(\theta))}\}
is not a Cartesian product. Under Assumption
1
(a)(b)(d)(e)(g) and (h), by Lemma
10
iii (p.
10
),
\tau:\mathbf{\Theta}\rightarrow\mathbf{R}^{m}
is continuous. Thus, by the topological definition of continuity,
\tau^{-1}[B_{\epsilon_{\mathbf{T}}/2}(\tau_{0})]
is an open set of
\mathbf{\Theta}
. Moreover, by the definition of
\tau_{0}
,
\theta_{0}\in\tau^{-1}[B_{\epsilon_{\mathbf{T}}/2}(\tau_{0})]
, and, by Assumption
1
(c),
\theta_{0}\in\mathrm{int}(\mathbf{\Theta})
,
13
13

13

This assumption forbids
\theta_{0}
to be on the boundary of
\tau^{-1}[B_{\epsilon_{\mathbf{T}}/2}(\tau_{0})]
, which is an open set of
\mathbf{\Theta}
, but not necessarily of
\mathbf{R}^{m}
.
so that there exists
r_{0}>0
s.t.
B_{r_{0}}(\theta_{0})\subset\tau^{-1}[B_{\epsilon_{\mathbf{T}}/2}(\tau_{0})]
and
B_{r_{0}}(\theta_{0})\subset\mathbf{\Theta}
.
Now, for this proof, put
r=\min\{r_{0},\epsilon_{\mathbf{T}}/2\}
. Then, it remains to show that
B_{r}(\theta_{0},\tau_{0})\subset\mathbf{S}
, i.e., for all
(\dot{\theta},\dot{\tau})\in B_{r}(\theta_{0},\tau_{0})
,
|\dot{\tau}-\tau(\dot{\theta})|\leqslant\epsilon_{\mathbf{T}}
. By the triangle inequality, for any
(\dot{\theta},\dot{\tau})\in B_{r}(\theta_{0},\tau_{0})
,

\displaystyle|\dot{\tau}-\tau(\dot{\theta})|

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle|\dot{\tau}-\tau_{0}|+|\tau_{0}-\tau(\dot{\theta})|

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon_{\mathbf{T}}}{2}+\frac{\epsilon_{\mathbf{T}}}{2}=\epsilon_{\mathbf{T}}

where the explanations for the last inequality are as follows. Firstly,
|\dot{\tau}-\tau_{0}|<\sqrt{\sum_{k=1}^{m}(\dot{\tau}_{k}-\tau_{0,k})^{2}}\leqslant\sqrt{\sum_{k=1}^{m}(\dot{\theta}_{k}-\theta_{0,k})^{2}+\sum_{k=1}^{m}(\dot{\tau}_{k}-\tau_{0,k})^{2}}<r\leqslant\frac{\epsilon_{\mathbf{T}}}{2}
by definition of
r
. Secondly, and similarly,
|\dot{\theta}-\theta_{0}|<\sqrt{\sum_{k=1}^{m}(\dot{\theta}_{k}-\theta_{0,k})^{2}}\leqslant\sqrt{\sum_{k=1}^{m}(\dot{\theta}_{k}-\theta_{0,k})^{2}+\sum_{k=1}^{m}(\dot{\tau}_{k}-\tau_{0,k})^{2}}<r\leqslant r_{0}
, so that
|\tau_{0}-\tau(\dot{\theta})|<\frac{\epsilon_{\mathbf{T}}}{2}
because
B_{r_{0}}(\theta_{0})\subset\tau^{-1}[B_{\epsilon_{\mathbf{T}}/2}(\tau_{0})]
.

(iii)

Under Assumption
1
(a)-(b) and (e)-(f), by Lemma
7
i (p.
7
), Assumption
1
(b) and the Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem,
(\theta,\tau)\mapsto\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\right]
is continuous in
\mathbf{S}
, and thus in a neighborhood of
(\theta_{0},\tau_{0})
in
\mathbf{S}
by Assumption
1
(c) and (e). Then,
(\theta,\tau)\mapsto|\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\right]|_{\det}^{2}
is also continuous. Now, by Assumption
1
(h),

|\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta_{0})^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}]|_{\det}^{2}>0
, so that, under Assumption
1
(a)-(e) and (g)-(h), by the statement (ii) of the present lemma, there exists a closed ball
\overline{B_{r_{\partial}}(\theta_{0},\tau_{0})}\subset\mathbf{S}
centered at
(\theta_{0},\tau_{0})
with radius
r_{\partial}>0
, s.t., for all
(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{r_{\partial}}(\theta_{0},\tau_{0})}
,
0<|\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\right]|_{\det}^{2}
, which is the first part of the result. By Lemma
30
(p.
30
), the second part of the result follows from the continuity of
(\theta,\tau)\mapsto\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\right]
, the invertibility of
\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\right]
for all
(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{r_{\partial}}(\theta_{0},\tau_{0})}
, and Lemma
7
ii (p.
7
), which, under Assumption
1
(a)-(b) and (e)-(f), implies that

\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{r_{\partial}}(\theta_{0},\tau_{0})}}|\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi_{t}(\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right]|=o(1)
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. as
T\rightarrow\infty
.

(iv)
It follows from Lemma
30
(p.
30
), so that it is sufficient to check its assumptions. Firstly, under Assumptions
1
(a)-(b), (e), (g) and (h), by Corollary
1
(p.
1
), for all
(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}
,
\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}\right]
is a positive definite symmetric matrix, and thus it is invertible. Secondly, under Assumption
1
(a)-(b), (e) and (g), by Lemma
8
i (p.
8
),

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}|\right]<\infty
, so that by the Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem and Assumption
1
(b),
(\theta,\tau)\mapsto\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}\right]
is continuous in
\mathbf{S}
. Finally, under Assumptions
1
(a)-(b), (d), (e), (g) and (h),
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. as
T\rightarrow\infty
,

\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\psi_{t}(\theta)\psi_{t}(\theta)^{\prime}-\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}]\right|=o(1)
.

∎

In order to simplify the analysis of the asymptotic properties of the ESP estimator, we decompose the LogESP into three terms.

Lemma 12

(LogESP decomposition)
.

Under Assumption
1
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for
T
big enough, define, for all
(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{r_{\partial}}(\theta_{0},\tau_{0})}
,

L_{T}(\theta,\tau):=\ln\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\right]\\
-\frac{1}{2T}\ln\negthickspace\left\{\left|\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}}{\sum_{i=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{i}(\theta)}}\frac{\partial\psi_{t}(\theta)}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\right]^{-1}\negthickspace\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}}{\sum_{i=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{i}(\theta)}}\psi_{t}(\theta)\psi_{t}(\theta)^{\prime}\right]\negthickspace\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}}{\sum_{i=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{i}(\theta)}}\frac{\partial\psi_{t}(\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right]^{-1}\right|_{\det}\negthickspace\right\}
, which exists by Lemma
11
(p.
11
), and where
\overline{B_{r_{\partial}}(\theta_{0},\tau_{0})}
is defined as in the aforementioned lemma. Then, under Assumption
1
(a)(b) and (d)-(h),
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for
T
big enough, for all
(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{r_{\partial}}(\theta_{0},\tau_{0})}
,

\displaystyle L_{T}(\theta,\tau)=M_{1,T}(\theta,\tau)+M_{2,T}(\theta,\tau)+M_{3,T}(\theta,\tau)\text{ where}

M_{1,T}(\theta,\tau)\negthickspace:=\negthickspace\left(1\negthickspace-\negthickspace\frac{m}{2T}\right)\ln\negthickspace\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\right]
,
M_{2,T}(\theta,\tau)\negthickspace:=\negthickspace\frac{1}{2T}\ln\negthickspace\left[\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi_{t}(\theta)}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\right|_{\det}^{2}\right]
, and

M_{3,T}(\theta,\tau)\negthickspace:=\negthickspace-\frac{1}{2T}\ln\negthickspace\left[\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\psi_{t}(\theta)\psi_{t}(\theta)^{\prime}\right|_{\det}\right]
.

Proof.

First of all, note that, under Assumption
1
, by Lemma
11
(p.
11
),
L_{T}(.)
is well-defined
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for
T
big enough, for all
(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{r_{\partial}}(\theta_{0},\tau_{0})}
. Thus, under Assumption
1
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for
T
big enough, for all
(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{r_{\partial}}(\theta_{0},\tau_{0})}
.

\displaystyle L_{T}(\theta,\tau)

(23)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ln\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\right]

\displaystyle\negthickspace-\frac{1}{2T}\ln\negthickspace\left\{\left|\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}}{\sum_{i=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{i}(\theta)}}\frac{\partial\psi_{t}(\theta)}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\right]^{-1}\negthickspace\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}}{\sum_{i=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{i}(\theta)}}\psi_{t}(\theta)\psi_{t}(\theta)^{\prime}\right]\right.\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 158.99377pt\left.\left.\times\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}}{\sum_{i=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{i}(\theta)}}\frac{\partial\psi_{t}(\theta)^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right]^{-1}\right|_{\det}\negthickspace\right\}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\ln\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\right]+\frac{1}{2T}\ln\left[\left|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}}{\sum_{i=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{i}(\theta)}}\frac{\partial\psi_{t}(\theta)}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\right|_{\det}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\ -\frac{1}{2T}\ln\left[\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}}{\sum_{i=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{i}(\theta)}}\psi_{t}(\theta)\psi_{t}(\theta)^{\prime}\right|_{\det}\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\ln\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2T}\ln\left[\left(\frac{1}{\frac{1}{T}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{i}(\theta)}}\right)^{2m}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi_{t}(\theta)}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\right|_{\det}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2T}\ln\left[\left(\frac{1}{\frac{1}{T}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{i}(\theta)}}\right)^{m}\left|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\psi_{t}(\theta)\psi_{t}(\theta)^{\prime}\right|_{\det}\right]\

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{m}{2T}\right)\ \ln\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\right]+\frac{1}{2T}\ln\left[\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi_{t}(\theta)}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\right|_{\det}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2T}\ln\left[\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\psi_{t}(\theta)\psi_{t}(\theta)^{\prime}\right|_{\det}\right].

(24)

(a)
Firstly, use that the determinant of the product is the product of the determinants
(e.g.
Rudin 1953
, Theorem 9.35)
. Secondly, the determinant of an inverse is the inverse of the determinant
(e.g.
Rudin 1953
, p. 233)
. Finally, use basic properties of the logarithm, and note that we keep the square in the second logarithm in order to ensure the positivity of the argument (then the strict positivity is ensured by Lemma
11
on p.
11
).
(b)
Use multilinearity of determinant.
(c)
Note that
1+\frac{-2m}{2T}-\frac{-m}{2T}=1-\frac{m}{2T}
.
∎

B.2.1.
Derivatives of
M_{1,T}(\theta,\tau):=\left(1-\frac{m}{2T}\right)\ln\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\right]

First derivative
\frac{\partial M_{1,T}(\theta,\tau)}{\partial\theta_{j}}
.

By Assumption
1
(b),
\theta\mapsto\psi(X_{1},\theta)
is differentiable in
\mathbf{\Theta}

\mathbb{P}
-a.s. Thus, for all
(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}
, for all
j\in[\![1,m]\!]
,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial M_{1,T}(\theta,\tau)}{\partial\theta_{j}}=\left(1-\frac{m}{2T}\right)\frac{\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial\psi_{t}(\theta)}{\partial\theta_{j}}}{\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}}.

(25)

Second derivative
\frac{\partial^{2}M_{1,T}(\theta,\tau)}{\partial\theta_{\ell}\partial\theta_{j}}

(a),

\theta_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(1-\frac{m}{2T})}{\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\right]^{2}}\left\{\left\{\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\left[\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial\psi_{t}(\theta)}{\partial\theta_{\ell}}\right]\left[\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial\psi_{t}(\theta)}{\partial\theta_{j}}\right]+\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\left[\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial^{2}\psi_{t}(\theta)}{\partial\theta_{j}\partial\theta_{\ell}}\right]\right\}\right.

\displaystyle\times\left\{\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\right\}-\left\{\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\left[\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial\psi_{t}(\theta)}{\partial\theta_{j}}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle\left.\times\left\{\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\left[\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial\psi_{t}(\theta)}{\partial\theta_{\ell}}\right]\right\}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(1-\frac{m}{2T})}{\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\right]}\left\{\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\left[\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial\psi_{t}(\theta)}{\partial\theta_{\ell}}\right]\left[\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial\psi_{t}(\theta)}{\partial\theta_{j}}\right]+\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\left[\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial^{2}\psi_{t}(\theta)}{\partial\theta_{j}\partial\theta_{\ell}}\right]\right\}

The trace of a derivative is the derivative of the trace because both the trace and differentiation operators are linear
(e.g.,
Magnus and Neudecker 1999/1988

. Moreover, if

(e.g.,
Magnus and Neudecker 1999/1988
, chap. 8 sec. 4)
. Now, under Assumption

),

B.3.
Proof of Theorem
1
(ii) : Asymptotic normality

The proof of Theorem
1
(ii) (i.e., asymptotic normality) adapts the traditional approach
of expanding the FOCs (first order conditions). The two main differences w.r.t. the proofs in the entropy literature are the following. Firstly, instead of expanding the FOC
\left.\frac{\partial L_{T}(\theta,\tau_{T}(\theta))}{\partial\theta}\right|_{\theta=\hat{\theta}_{T}}
, we expand the approximate FOC
\left.\frac{\partial L_{T}(\theta,\tau)}{\partial\theta}\right|_{(\theta,\tau)=(\hat{\theta}_{T},\tau_{T}(\hat{\theta}_{T}))}
combined with the FOC (
15
) for
\tau
on p.
15
. Secondly, we need to control the asymptotic behaviour of the derivatives that come from
\ln|\Sigma_{T}(\theta)|_{\det}
.

Core of the proof of Theorem
1
(ii).

We prove asymptotic normality adapting the traditional approach of expanding the FOCs (first order conditions). Note that our approximate FOCs are written as a functionof the
2m
variables
\theta
and
\tau
. In other words, instead of using the implicit function
\tau_{T}(\theta),

\tau
is an estimated parameter and hence the ET equation (
15
) on p.
15
is also included in the expansion.

Under Assumptions
1
and
2
, by Proposition
1
(p.
1
),
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. as
T\rightarrow\infty
,

\displaystyle\sqrt{T}\left[\begin{array}[]{c}\left(\hat{\theta}_{T}-\theta_{0}\right)\\
\tau_{T}(\hat{\theta}_{T})\end{array}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\begin{bmatrix}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\right]^{-1}\\
0_{m\times m}\\
\end{bmatrix}\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\psi_{t}(\theta_{0})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

\displaystyle\underset{(a)}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle D}}{{\rightarrow}}}

\displaystyle-\begin{bmatrix}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\right]^{-1}\\
0_{m\times m}\\
\end{bmatrix}\mathcal{N}(0,\mathbb{E}\left[\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})^{\prime}\right])

\displaystyle\underset{(b)}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle D}}{{=}}}

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}\left(0,\begin{bmatrix}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\right]^{-1}\\
0_{m\times m}\\
\end{bmatrix}\mathbb{E}\left[\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})^{\prime}\right]\begin{bmatrix}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right]^{-1}&0_{m\times m}\\
\end{bmatrix}\right)

\displaystyle\underset{}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle D}}{{=}}}

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}\left(0,\left(\begin{array}[]{c c }\Sigma(\theta_{0})&0_{m\times m}\\
0_{m\times m}&0_{m\times m}\end{array}\right)\right)\text{ }

where
\Sigma(\theta_{0})=\left[\mathbb{E}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\right]^{-1}\negthickspace\mathbb{E}\left[\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})^{\prime}\right]\left[\mathbb{E}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right]^{-1}
.
(a)
Under Assumption
1
(a)-(c) and (g), by the Lindeberg-Lévy CLT theorem,

\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\psi_{t}(\theta_{0})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle D}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{N}(0,\mathbb{E}\left[\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})^{\prime}]\right)
, as
T\rightarrow\infty
.
(b)
Firstly, the
minus sign can be discarded because of the symmetry of the Gaussian distribution. Secondly, if
X
is a random vector and
F
is a (deterministic) matrix, then
\mathbb{V}(FX)=F\mathbb{V}(X)F^{\prime}
.
∎

Proposition 1

(Asymptotic expansion of

.

0_{m\times m}\\
\end{bmatrix}\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\psi_{t}(\theta_{0})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

Proof.

The function
L_{T}(\theta,\tau)
is well-defined and twice continuously differentiable in a neighborhood of
(\theta_{0}^{\prime}\;\tau(\theta_{0})^{\prime})

\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for

big enough by subsection

(p.
B.2
), under Assumptions

and

(a). Similarly, let

(a)(b). Now, under Assumption

\mathbb{P}

. Therefore, under Assumption

(a), a stochastic first-order Taylor-Lagrange expansion
(

, Lemma 3)
around

\displaystyle\left[\begin{array}[]{c}\frac{\partial{L}_{T}(\hat{\theta}_{T},\tau_{T}(\hat{\theta}_{T}))}{\partial\theta}\\

S_{T}(\theta_{0},\tau(\theta_{0}))\end{array}\right]+\left[\begin{array}[]{c c }\frac{\partial^{2}{L}_{T}(\bar{\theta}_{T},\bar{\tau}_{T})}{\partial\theta^{\prime}\partial\theta}&\frac{\partial^{2}{L}_{T}(\bar{\theta}_{T},\bar{\tau}_{T})}{\partial\tau^{\prime}\partial\theta}\\

and

and

, respectively. Moreover, under Assumptions

\mathbb{P}

\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle\sqrt{T}\left[\begin{array}[]{c}\left(\hat{\theta}_{T}-\theta_{0}\right)\\
\tau_{T}(\hat{\theta}_{T})\end{array}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left[\begin{array}[]{c c }\frac{\partial^{2}{L}_{T}(\bar{\theta}_{T},\bar{\tau}_{T})}{\partial\theta^{\prime}\partial\theta}&\frac{\partial^{2}{L}_{T}(\bar{\theta}_{T},\bar{\tau}_{T})}{\partial\tau^{\prime}\partial\theta}\\

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{=}}

\sqrt{T}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\psi_{t}(\theta_{0})\end{array}\right]

\displaystyle-\left\{\left[\begin{array}[]{c c }\frac{\partial^{2}{L}_{T}(\bar{\theta}_{T},\bar{\tau}_{T})}{\partial\theta^{\prime}\partial\theta}&\frac{\partial^{2}{L}_{T}(\bar{\theta}_{T},\bar{\tau}_{T})}{\partial\tau^{\prime}\partial\theta}\\
\frac{\partial S_{T}(\bar{\theta}_{T},\bar{\tau}_{T})}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}&\frac{\partial S_{T}(\bar{\theta}_{T},\bar{\tau}_{T})}{\partial\tau^{\prime}}\end{array}\right]^{-1}-\left[\begin{array}[]{c c }-\Sigma(\theta_{0})&\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\right]^{-1}\\
\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right]^{-1}&\ 0_{m\times m}\end{array}\right]\right\}\left[\begin{array}[]{c}O(T^{-\frac{1}{2}})\\

Remark 2

(Alternative approximate FOC)
.

In the proof of Theorem
1
ii, it is possible to use the approximate FOC
\frac{\partial M_{1,T}(\hat{\theta}_{T},\tau_{T}(\hat{\theta}_{T}))}{\partial\theta}=O(T^{-1})
instead of the approximate FOC
\frac{\partial{L}_{T}(\hat{\theta}_{T},\tau_{T}(\hat{\theta}_{T}))}{\partial\theta}=O(T^{-1})
. Under Assumption
1
and
2
(with
k_{2}\in[\![1,3]\!]
and
j\in[\![0,2]\!]
in its part b), by Lemma
12
(p.
12
) and
18
v-vii,xii-xiv (p.
18
) and the ULLN à la Wald,
\frac{\partial{L}_{T}(\hat{\theta}_{T},\tau_{T}(\hat{\theta}_{T}))}{\partial\theta}=\frac{\partial M_{1,T}(\hat{\theta}_{T},\tau_{T}(\hat{\theta}_{T}))}{\partial\theta}+\frac{\partial M_{2,T}(\hat{\theta}_{T},\tau_{T}(\hat{\theta}_{T}))}{\partial\theta}+\frac{\partial M_{3,T}(\hat{\theta}_{T},\tau_{T}(\hat{\theta}_{T}))}{\partial\theta}=\frac{\partial M_{1,T}(\hat{\theta}_{T},\tau_{T}(\hat{\theta}_{T}))}{\partial\theta}+O(T^{-1})
. The approximate FOC
\frac{\partial M_{1,T}(\hat{\theta}_{T},\tau_{T}(\hat{\theta}_{T}))}{\partial\theta}=O(T^{-1})
would lead to replace expansion (
B.3
) on p.
B.3
with the following expansion

\displaystyle\left[\begin{array}[]{c}\frac{\partial M_{1,T}(\hat{\theta}_{T},\tau_{T}(\hat{\theta}_{T}))}{\partial\theta}\\
S_{T}(\hat{\theta}_{T},\tau_{T}(\hat{\theta}_{T}))\end{array}\right]=\left[\begin{array}[]{c}\frac{\partial M_{1,T}(\theta_{0},\tau(\theta_{0}))}{\partial\theta}\\
S_{T}(\theta_{0},\tau(\theta_{0}))\end{array}\right]+\left[\begin{array}[]{c c }\frac{\partial^{2}M_{1,T}(\bar{\theta}_{T},\bar{\tau}_{T})}{\partial\theta^{\prime}\partial\theta}&\frac{\partial^{2}M_{1,T}(\bar{\theta}_{T},\bar{\tau}_{T})}{\partial\tau^{\prime}\partial\theta}\\
\frac{\partial S_{T}(\bar{\theta}_{T},\bar{\tau}_{T})}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}&\frac{\partial S_{T}(\bar{\theta}_{T},\bar{\tau}_{T})}{\partial\tau^{\prime}}\end{array}\right]\left[\begin{array}[]{c}\left(\hat{\theta}_{T}-\theta_{0}\right)\\
\tau_{T}(\hat{\theta}_{T})\end{array}\right]

where
\frac{\partial^{2}M_{1,T}(\bar{\theta}_{T},\bar{\tau}_{T})}{\partial\theta^{\prime}\partial\theta}
and
\frac{\partial^{2}M_{1,T}(\bar{\theta}_{T},\bar{\tau}_{T})}{\partial\theta^{\prime}\partial\theta}
can easily be controlled by Lemma
18
i-iv (p.
18
), Lemma
19
i-v (p.
19
), Lemma
23
i-iii (p.
23
) and ULLN à la Wald under Assumptions
1
and
2
(with
k_{2}\in[\![1,3]\!]
and
j\in[\![0,2]\!]
in its part b). The approximate FOC
\frac{\partial M_{1,T}(\hat{\theta}_{T},\tau_{T}(\hat{\theta}_{T}))}{\partial\theta}=O(T^{-1})
requires less assumptions than the approximate FOC
\frac{\partial{L}_{T}(\hat{\theta}_{T},\tau_{T}(\hat{\theta}_{T}))}{\partial\theta}=O(T^{-1})
because it does not require to control the 2nd derivatives of
M_{2,T}(\theta,\tau)
and
M_{3,T}(\theta,\tau)
. However, it would not save space and it would require to add one more block of assumptions because our proof of Theorem
2
requires the full Assumption
2
.

\diamond

Lemma 13
.

Under Assumptions
1
and
2
,

(i)

for any sequence
(\theta_{T},\tau_{T})_{T\in\mathbf{N}}
converging to
(\theta_{0},\tau(\theta_{0}))
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. as
T\rightarrow\infty
,

\left[\begin{array}[]{c c }\frac{\partial^{2}{L}_{T}({\theta}_{T},{\tau}_{T})}{\partial\theta^{\prime}\partial\theta}&\frac{\partial^{2}{L}_{T}({\theta}_{T},{\tau}_{T})}{\partial\tau^{\prime}\partial\theta}\\
\frac{\partial S_{T}({\theta}_{T},{\tau}_{T})}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}&\frac{\partial S_{T}({\theta}_{T},{\tau}_{T})}{\partial\tau^{\prime}}\end{array}\right]\rightarrow\left[\begin{array}[]{c c }0_{m\times m}&\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\right]^{\prime}\\
\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\right]&\ \mathbb{E}\left[\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})^{\prime}\right]\end{array}\right]
;

(ii)

\left[\begin{array}[]{c c }0_{m\times m}&\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\right]^{\prime}\\
\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\right]&\ \mathbb{E}\left[\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})^{\prime}\right]\end{array}\right]
is invertible, so that, for any sequence
(\theta_{T},\tau_{T})_{T\in\mathbf{N}}
converging to
(\theta_{0},\tau(\theta_{0}))
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s., for
T
big enough, the matrix
\left[\begin{array}[]{c c }\frac{\partial^{2}{L}_{T}({\theta}_{T},{\tau}_{T})}{\partial\theta^{\prime}\partial\theta}&\frac{\partial^{2}{L}_{T}({\theta}_{T},{\tau}_{T})}{\partial\tau^{\prime}\partial\theta}\\
\frac{\partial S_{T}({\theta}_{T},{\tau}_{T})}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}&\frac{\partial S_{T}({\theta}_{T},{\tau}_{T})}{\partial\tau^{\prime}}\end{array}\right]
is invertible; and

(iii)

for any sequence
(\theta_{T},\tau_{T})_{T\in\mathbf{N}}
converging to
(\theta_{0},\tau(\theta_{0}))
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. as
T\rightarrow\infty
,

\left[\begin{array}[]{c c }\frac{\partial^{2}{L}_{T}(\theta_{T},\tau_{T})}{\partial\theta^{\prime}\partial\theta}&\frac{\partial^{2}{L}_{T}(\theta_{T},\tau_{T})}{\partial\tau^{\prime}\partial\theta}\\
\frac{\partial S_{T}(\theta_{T},\tau_{T})}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}&\frac{\partial S_{T}(\theta_{T},\tau_{T})}{\partial\tau^{\prime}}\end{array}\right]^{-1}\rightarrow\left[\begin{array}[]{c c }-\Sigma(\theta_{0})&\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\right]^{-1}\\
\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right]^{-1}&\ 0_{m\times m}\end{array}\right]
, where

\displaystyle\left[\begin{array}[]{c c }-\Sigma(\theta_{0})&\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\right]^{-1}\\
\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right]^{-1}&\ 0_{m\times m}\end{array}\right]=\left[\begin{array}[]{c c }0_{m\times m}&\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\right]^{\prime}\\
\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\right]&\ \mathbb{E}\left[\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})^{\prime}\right]\end{array}\right]^{-1}

.

Proof.

(i)
Under Assumptions
1
and
2
, it follows from Lemma
14
ii and iii (p.
14
) and Lemma
17
(p.
17
), given that
\tau(\theta_{0})=0_{m\times 1}
by Lemma
10
ii (p.
10
) and Assumption
1
(c), under Assumption
1
(a)(b)(d)(e)(g) and (h).

(ii)

Assumption
1
(h) implies the invertibility of

\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta_{0})^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})}\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})^{\prime}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})^{\prime}\right]
and
\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau(\theta_{0})^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\right]
because
\tau(\theta_{0})=0_{m\times 1}
by Lemma
10
iv (p.
10
) under Assumption
1
(a)-(e)(g)-(h).
Thus,
\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})^{\prime}\right]^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\right]
is also invertible, so that the first part of the statement (ii) follows from Lemma
33
ii (p.
33
) with
A=0_{m\times m}
,
B=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right]
,

and
D=\mathbb{E}\left[\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})^{\prime}\right]
. Then, the second
part of the statement follows from a trivial case of the Lemma
30
(p.
30
).

(iii)
Under under Assumption
1
(a)(b)(c)(d)(e)(g)(h), by the statement (ii) of the present lemma, the limiting matrix is invertible. Thus, by the inverse formula for partitioned matrices
(e.g.,
Magnus and Neudecker 1999/1988
, Chap. 1 Sec. 11)
,

\displaystyle\left[\begin{array}[]{c c }0_{m\times m}&\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\right]^{\prime}\\
\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\right]&\ \mathbb{E}\left[\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})^{\prime}\right]\end{array}\right]^{-1}=\left[\begin{array}[]{c c }-\Sigma(\theta_{0})&\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\right]^{-1}\\
\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})^{\prime}}{\partial\theta}\right]^{-1}&\ 0_{m\times 1}\end{array}\right]

because
(-M^{\prime}V^{-1}M)^{-1}=-M^{-1}V(M^{\prime})^{-1}:=-\Sigma(\theta_{0})
. Then, the result follows from the continuity of the inverse transformation
(e.g.,
Rudin 1953
, Theorem 9.8)
.
∎

Lemma 14
.

Under Assumptions
1
and
2
,

(i)

\mathbb{P}
-a.s. as

,

T\frac{\partial L_{T}(\theta_{0},\tau(\theta_{0}))}{\partial\theta_{j}}\rightarrow{\rm tr}\left\{\left[\mathbb{E}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\right]^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\partial^{2}\psi_{t}(\theta_{0})}{\partial\theta_{j}\partial\theta^{\prime}}\right]\right\}-\frac{1}{2}{\rm tr}\Big\{\left[\mathbb{E}\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})^{\prime}\right]^{-1}\\
\times\Big[\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})}{\partial\theta_{j}}\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})^{\prime}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta_{0})}{\partial\theta_{j}}^{\prime}\right]\Big]\Big\}

;

for any sequence

Proof.

(65)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hskip 7.22743pt-\frac{1}{2T}{\rm tr}\left\{\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\psi_{t}(\theta_{0})\psi_{t}(\theta_{0})^{\prime}\right]^{-1}\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\negthickspace\left\{\frac{\partial\psi_{t}(\theta_{0})}{\partial\theta_{j}}\psi_{t}(\theta_{0})^{\prime}\negthickspace+\negthickspace\psi_{t}(\theta_{0})\frac{\partial\psi_{t}(\theta_{0})^{\prime}}{\partial\theta_{j}}\right\}\right]\right\}.

Now, under Assumption

(a)(b),

),

;

; and

under additional Assumption

(f)(g), by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the monotonicity of integration,

\mathbb{P}
-a.s. as

.

1
and
2
, for all

,
\mathbb{P}

,

.

(ii)
Under Assumptions
1
and
2
, by Lemma

(p.

) and Lemma

(p.

),
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. as

, uniformly over a closed ball around

-\frac{1\frac{}{}}{\left[\mathbb{E}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\right]^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{j}}\right]\times\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{\ell}}\right]
. Now, under Assumption
1
(a)(b)(d) (e)(g) and (h), by Lemma

ii (p.

) and Assumption

(c), put
\tau(\theta_{0})=0_{m\times 1}
, so that the result follows.

(iii)
Under Assumptions
1
and
2
, by Lemma
16
(p.
16
) and Lemma
12
(p.
12
),
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. as
T\rightarrow\infty
, uniformly over a closed ball around
(\theta_{0},\tau(\theta_{0}))
with strictly positive radius,
\frac{\partial^{2}L_{T}(\theta,\tau)}{\partial\tau_{k}\partial\theta_{\ell}}\rightarrow\frac{1}{\left[\mathbb{E}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\right]^{2}}\times\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\right]\mathbb{E}\left\{\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{\ell}}\psi_{k}(X_{1,}\theta)+\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi_{k}(X_{1,}\theta)}{\partial\theta_{\ell}}\right\}\right.\\
\left.\hskip 36.135pt-\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{\ell}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi_{k}(X_{1,}\theta)\right]\right\}
. Now, under Assumption
1
(a)(b)(d)(e)(g) and (h), by Lemma
10
ii (p.
10
) and Assumption
1
(c),
\tau(\theta_{0})=0_{m\times 1}
, so that
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. as
T\rightarrow\infty
,
\frac{\partial^{2}L_{T}(\theta_{T},\tau_{T})}{\partial\tau_{k}\partial\theta_{\ell}}\rightarrow\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\partial\psi_{k}(X_{1,}\theta)}{\partial\theta_{\ell}}\right]
. Stack the components together in order to obtain the result.

∎

Lemma 15

(Uniform limit of
\frac{\partial^{2}L_{T}(\theta,\tau)}{\partial\theta_{j}\partial\theta_{\ell}}
in a neighborhood of
(\theta_{0},\tau(\theta_{0}))
)
.

Under Assumptions
1
and
2
, for all
(j,\ell)\in[\![1,m]\!]^{2}
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. as
T\rightarrow\infty
, uniformly over a closed ball around
(\theta_{0},\tau(\theta_{0}))
with strictly positive radius,

(i)

\frac{\partial^{2}M_{1,T}(\theta,\tau)}{\partial\theta_{j}\partial\theta_{\ell}}\rightarrow\frac{1}{\left[\mathbb{E}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\right]}\mathbb{E}\left\{\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\left[\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{\ell}}\right]\left[\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{j}}\right]+\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\left[\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial^{2}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{j}\partial\theta_{\ell}}\right]\right\}\\
-\frac{1\frac{}{}}{\left[\mathbb{E}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\right]^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{j}}\right]\times\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{\ell}}\right]
;

(ii)

\frac{\partial^{2}M_{2,T}(\theta,\tau)}{\partial\theta_{j}\partial\theta_{\ell}}\rightarrow 0
;

(iii)

\frac{\partial^{2}M_{3,T}(\theta,\tau)}{\partial\theta_{j}\partial\theta_{\ell}}\rightarrow 0
.

Proof.

(i)
Under Assumptions
1
and
2
, by Lemma
18
i-iv (p.
18
), Assumption
1
(a) and (b), all the averages in
\frac{\partial^{2}M_{1,T}(\theta,\tau)}{\partial\theta_{j}\partial\theta_{\ell}}
(equation (
26
) on p.

) satisfy the assumptions of the ULLN à la Wald. Moreover, under Assumption
1
(a)-(b) (d)(e)(g) and (h), by Lemma
11
i (p.
11
) the averages in the denominators are bounded away from zero. Thus, the result follows from the ULLN à la Wald. Note that the coefficient
\frac{m}{2T}
vanishes as it goes to zero, as
T\rightarrow\infty
.

(ii)
Under Assumptions
1
and
2
, by Lemma
18
v-xi (p.

), Assumption

(a) and (b), all the averages in
\frac{\partial^{2}M_{2,T}(\theta,\tau)}{\partial\theta_{j}\partial\theta_{\ell}}
(equation (

) on p.

) satisfy the assumptions of the ULLN à la Wald. Moreover, under Assumption

, by Lemma

iii (p.

) the averages in the inverted matrices are invertible in a neighborhood of
(\theta_{0},\tau(\theta_{0}))

\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for
T
big enough. Thus, the result follows from the ULLN à la Wald, the linearity of the trace operator and the scaling by
\frac{1}{T}
.

(iii)
Under Assumptions

and

, by Lemma

xii-xix (p.

), Assumption

) the averages in the inverted matrices are invertible in a neighborhood of
(\theta_{0},\tau(\theta_{0}))
,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. for

big enough. Thus, the result follows from the ULLN à la Wald, the linearity of the trace operator and the scaling by
\frac{1}{T}
.
∎

Lemma 17
.

Put
S_{T}(\theta,\tau):=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\psi_{t}(\theta)
. Under Assumptions
1
and
2
, there exists a closed ball centered at
(\theta_{0}^{\prime}\,\tau(\theta_{0})^{\prime})
with strictly positive radius s.t.,
\mathbb{P}
-a.s. as
T\rightarrow\infty
,

(i)

\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{r_{L}}((\theta_{0},\tau_{0}))}}\left|\frac{\partial S_{T}(\theta,\tau)}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\right]\right|=o(1)
;

(ii)

\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{r_{L}}((\theta_{0},\tau_{0}))}}\left|\frac{\partial S_{T}(\theta,\tau)}{\partial\tau^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}\right]\right|=o(1)
.

Proof.

(i)
By definition of
S_{T}(\theta,\tau)
,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial S(\theta,\tau)}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\psi_{t}(\theta)\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial\psi_{t}(\theta)}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}+\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi_{t}(\theta)}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}.

Thus, by the triangle inequality,

\displaystyle\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{r_{L}}((\theta_{0},\tau_{0}))}}\left|\frac{\partial S_{T}(\theta,\tau)}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{r_{L}}((\theta_{0},\tau_{0}))}}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\psi_{t}(\theta)\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial\psi_{t}(\theta)}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\right]\right|

\displaystyle+\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{r_{L}}((\theta_{0},\tau_{0}))}}\left|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi_{t}(\theta)}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\right]\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(1)\text{ $\mathbb{P}$-a.s. as $T\rightarrow\infty$}

where the last equality follows from the ULLN à la Wald by Assumption
1
(a)(b) and Lemma
18
iv-v (p.
18
), under Assumptions
1
and
2
.

(ii)
By definition of
S_{T}(\theta,\tau)
,
\frac{\partial S_{T}(\theta,\tau)}{\partial\tau^{\prime}}=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi_{t}(\theta)}\psi_{t}(\theta)\psi_{t}(\theta)^{\prime}
. Now, under Assumption
1
(a)-(b)(e) and (g), by Lemma
8
i (p.
8
) and Assumption
1
(a)(b), the assumptions of the ULLN à la Wald are satisfied, so that the result follows from the latter.
∎

Lemma 18

(Finiteness of the expectations of the supremum of the terms from
\frac{\partial^{2}L_{T}(\theta,\tau)}{\partial\theta_{\ell}\partial\theta_{j}}
)
.

Under Assumptions
1
and
2
, there exists a closed ball
\overline{B_{L}}\subset\mathbf{S}
centered at
(\theta_{0},\tau(\theta_{0}))
with strictly positive radius s.t., for all
(\ell,j)\in[\![1,m]\!]^{2}
,

(i)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1,}\theta)}\right]<\infty
;

(ii)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{\ell}}\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{j}}|\right]<\infty
;

(iii)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial^{2}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{j}\partial\theta_{\ell}}|\right]<\infty
;

(iv)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{\ell}}|\right]<\infty
;

(v)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}|\right]<\infty
;

(vi)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial^{2}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{l}\partial\theta^{\prime}}|\right]<\infty
;

(vii)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{\ell}}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}|\right]<\infty
;

(viii)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial^{3}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{l}\partial\theta_{j}\partial\theta^{\prime}}|\right]<\infty
;

(ix)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{\ell}}\frac{\partial^{2}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{j}\partial\theta^{\prime}}|\right]<\infty
;

(x)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial^{2}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{\ell}\partial\theta_{j}}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}|\right]<\infty
;

(xi)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{\ell}}\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{j}}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}|\right]<\infty
;

(xii)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}|\right]<\infty
;

(xiii)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{\ell}}\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}|\right]<\infty
;

(xiv)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{\ell}}\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}|\right]<\infty
;

(xv)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial^{2}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{l}\partial\theta_{j}}\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}|\right]<\infty
;

(xvi)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{\ell}}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{j}}|\right]<\infty
;

(xvii)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}(\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{\ell}})\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{j}}\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}|\right]<\infty
;

(xviii)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}(\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial^{2}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{\ell}\partial\theta_{j}})\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}|\right]<\infty
; and

(xix)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}(\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{\ell}})(\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{j}})\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}|\right]<\infty
.

Proof.

(i)
Under Assumption
1
(a)-(e) and (g)-(h), by Lemma
11
ii (p.
11
),
\mathbf{S}
contains an open ball centered at
(\theta_{0},\tau(\theta_{0}))
, so that, by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, for
\overline{B_{L}}
of sufficiently small radius,
\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1,}\theta)}\right]\leqslant\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[(\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1,}\theta)})^{2}\right]}=\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\mathbf{S}}\mathrm{e}^{2\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1,}\theta)}\right]}<\infty
where the equality follows from the fact that supremum of the square of a positive function is the square of the supremum of the function, and the last inequality from Assumption
1
(e).

(ii)
The norm of a product of matrices is smaller than the product of the norms
(e.g.,
Rudin 1953
, Theorem 9.7 and note that all norms are equivalent on finite dimensional spaces)
. Thus,

for
\overline{B_{L}}
of sufficiently small radius, for all
(\ell,j)\in[\![1,m]\!]^{2}
,

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{\ell}}\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{j}}|\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle(\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\tau|^{2})\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}|\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{\ell}}||\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{j}}|\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leqslant}}

\displaystyle(\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\tau|^{2})\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\theta\in\mathcal{N}}\sup_{\tau\in\mathbf{T}(\theta)}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}b(X_{1})^{2}\right]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{<}}\infty.

(a)
Firstly, under Assumption
1
(a)-(e) and (g)-(h), by Lemma
11
ii (p.
11
),
\mathbf{S}
contains an open ball centered at
(\theta_{0},\tau(\theta_{0}))
Thus, under Assumption
1
(a)-(e) and (g)-(h), for
\overline{B_{L}}
of sufficiently small radius, by definition of
\mathbf{S}
,
\overline{B_{L}}\subset\{(\theta,\tau):\theta\in\mathcal{N}\wedge\tau\in\mathbf{T}(\theta)\}\subset\mathbf{S}
, because
\mathcal{N}\subset\mathbf{\Theta}
by Assumption
2
(a). Secondly, by Assumption
2
(b),
\sup_{\theta\in\mathcal{N}}|\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{\ell}}|\leqslant b(X)
and
\sup_{\theta\in\mathcal{N}}|\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{j}}|\leqslant b(X)
.
(b)
Firstly,
\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\tau|^{2}<\infty
because
\overline{B_{L}}
is bounded. Secondly, by Assumption
2
(b),
\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\theta\in\mathcal{N}}\sup_{\tau\in\mathbf{T}(\theta)}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}b(X_{1})^{2}\right]<\infty
.

(iii)
Similarly to the proof of statement (ii), under Assumption
1
(a)-(e) and (g)-(h), for
\overline{B_{L}}
of sufficiently small radius, for all
(\ell,j)\in[\![1,m]\!]^{2}
,

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial^{2}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{j}\partial\theta_{\ell}}|\right]\leqslant(\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\tau|)\\
\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}|\frac{\partial^{2}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{j}\partial\theta_{\ell}}|\right]\leqslant(\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\tau|)\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\theta\in\mathcal{N}}\sup_{\tau\in\mathbf{T}(\theta)}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}b(X_{1})\right]<\infty
, where the two last inequalities follow from Assumption
2
(b) and the boundedness of
\overline{B_{L}}
.

(iv)
Similarly to the proof of statement (ii), under Assumption
1
(a)-(e) and (g)-(h), for
\overline{B_{L}}
of sufficiently small radius, for all
\ell\in[\![1,m]\!]
,

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{\ell}}|\right]\leqslant(\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\tau|)\\
\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}|\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{\ell}}|\right]\leqslant(\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\tau|)\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\theta\in\mathcal{N}}\sup_{\tau\in\mathbf{T}(\theta)}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}b(X_{1})\right]<\infty
, where the two last inequalities follow from Assumption
2
(b) and the boundedness of
\overline{B_{L}}
.

(v)
Similarly to the proof of statement (ii), under Assumption
1
(a)-(e) and (g)-(h), for
\overline{B_{L}}
of sufficiently small radius,
\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}|\right]\leqslant\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\theta\in\mathcal{N}}\sup_{\tau\in\mathbf{T}(\theta)}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}b(X_{1})\right]<\infty
, where the last inequality follows from Assumption
2
(b).

(vi)
Similarly to the proof of statement (ii), under Assumption
1
(a)-(e) and (g)-(h), for
\overline{B_{L}}
of sufficiently small radius, for all
\ell\in[\![1,m]\!]
,
\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial^{2}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{\ell}\partial\theta^{\prime}}|\right]\\
\leqslant\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\theta\in\mathcal{N}}\sup_{\tau\in\mathbf{T}(\theta)}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}b(X_{1})\right]<\infty
, where the last inequality follows from Assumption

(xiii)

The supremum of the absolute value of the product is smaller than the product of the
suprema of the absolute values. Thus,
under Assumption
1
(a)(b), for
\overline{B_{L}}
of sufficiently small radius, for all
\ell\in[\![1,m]\!]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{\ell}}\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}|\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{\ell}}|\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}|\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leqslant}}

\displaystyle\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{\ell}}|^{2}\right]}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}|^{2}\right]}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leqslant}}

\displaystyle\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\theta\in\mathcal{N}}\sup_{\tau\in\mathbf{T}(\theta)}\mathrm{e}^{2\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}b(X_{1})^{2}\right]}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}^{\epsilon}}|\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}|^{2}\right]}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{<}}\infty.

(a)
Apply the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, and note that the supremum of the square of a positive function is the square of the supremum of the function.
(b)
Firstly, under Assumption
1
(a)-(e) and (g)-(h), by Lemma
11
ii (p.
11
),
\mathbf{S}
contains an open ball centered at
(\theta_{0},\tau(\theta_{0}))
, so that, for
\overline{B_{L}}
of sufficiently small radius,
\overline{B_{L}}\subset\{(\theta,\tau):\theta\in\mathcal{N}\wedge\tau\in\mathbf{T}(\theta)\}\subset\mathbf{S}\subset\mathbf{S}^{\epsilon}
because
\mathcal{N}\subset\mathbf{\Theta}
and
\mathbf{S}=\{(\theta,\tau):\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}\wedge\tau\in\mathbf{T}(\theta)\}
. Secondly, as the second supremum does not depend on
\tau
,
\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}|^{2}\leqslant\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}^{\epsilon}}|\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}|^{2}
because
\overline{B_{L}}\subset\mathbf{S}
, for
\overline{B_{L}}
of radius small enough.
(c)
By Assumption
2
(b), the first expectation is bounded. Under Assumption
1
(a)(b)(g), by Lemma
9
i (p.
9
), the second expectation is also bounded.

(xvii)
Proof similar to the one of statement (xiii). The norm of a product of matrices is smaller than the product of the norms
(e.g.,
Rudin 1953
, Theorem 9.7 and note that all norms are equivalent on finite dimensional spaces)
. Moreover, the supremum of the absolute value of the product is smaller than the product of the
suprema of the absolute values. Thus,
under Assumption
1
(a)(b), for
\overline{B_{L}}
of sufficiently small radius, for all
(\ell,j)\in[\![1,m]\!]^{2}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}(\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{\ell}})\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{j}}\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}|\right]<\infty

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle(\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\tau|)\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}\left(\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}|\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{\ell}}||\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{j}}|\right)\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\psi(X_{1},\theta)|\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leqslant}}

\displaystyle(\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\tau|)\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}\left(\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}|\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{\ell}}||\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{j}}|\right)^{2}\right]}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\psi(X_{1},\theta)|^{2}\right]}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leqslant}}

\displaystyle(\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\tau|)\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\theta\in\mathcal{N}}\sup_{\tau\in\mathbf{T}(\theta)}\mathrm{e}^{2\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}b(X_{1})^{4}\right]}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}^{\epsilon}}|\psi(X_{1},\theta)|^{2}\right]}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{<}}\infty.

(a)
Apply the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, and note that the supremum of the square of a positive function is the square of the supremum of the function.
(b)
Firstly, under Assumption
1
(a)-(e) and (g)-(h), by Lemma
11
ii (p.
11
),
\mathbf{S}
contains an open ball centered at
(\theta_{0},\tau(\theta_{0}))
, so that, for
\overline{B_{L}}
of sufficiently small radius,
\overline{B_{L}}\subset\{(\theta,\tau):\theta\in\mathcal{N}\wedge\tau\in\mathbf{T}(\theta)\}\subset\mathbf{S}\subset\mathbf{S}^{\epsilon}
. Secondly, as the second supremum does not depend on
\tau
,
\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}|^{2}\leqslant\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}^{\epsilon}}|\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}|^{2}
because
\overline{B_{L}}\subset\mathbf{S}
, for
\overline{B_{L}}
of radius small enough.
(c)
Firstly, because
\overline{B_{L}}
is bounded,
(\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\tau|)<\infty
. Secondly, by Assumption
2
(b), the first expectation is bounded. Thirdly, under Assumption
1
(a)(b)(g), by Lemma
9
(p.
9
), the second expectation is also bounded.

\displaystyle(\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\tau|^{2})\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}\left(\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}|\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{\ell}}||\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{j}}|\right)^{2}\right]}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}|^{2}\right]}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leqslant}}

\displaystyle(\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\tau|^{2})\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\theta\in\mathcal{N}}\sup_{\tau\in\mathbf{T}(\theta)}\mathrm{e}^{2\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}b(X_{1})^{4}\right]}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}^{\epsilon}}|\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}|^{2}\right]}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{<}}\infty.

(a)
Apply the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, and note that the supremum of the square of a positive function is the square of the supremum of the function.
(b)
Firstly, under Assumption
1
(a)-(e) and (g)-(h), by Lemma
11
ii (p.
11
),
\mathbf{S}
contains an open ball centered at
(\theta_{0},\tau(\theta_{0}))
, so that, for
\overline{B_{L}}
of sufficiently small radius,
\overline{B_{L}}\subset\{(\theta,\tau):\theta\in\mathcal{N}\wedge\tau\in\mathbf{T}(\theta)\}\subset\mathbf{S}\subset\mathbf{S}^{\epsilon}
. Secondly, as the second supremum does not depend on
\tau
,
\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}|^{2}\leqslant\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}^{\epsilon}}|\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}|^{2}
because
\overline{B_{L}}\subset\mathbf{S}
, for
\overline{B_{L}}
of radius small enough.
(c)
Firstly, because
\overline{B_{L}}
is bounded,
(\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\tau|^{2})<\infty
. Secondly, by Assumption
2
(b), the first expectation is bounded. Thirdly, by Assumption
1
(g), the second expectation is also bounded.
∎

Lemma 19

(Finiteness of the expectations of the supremum of the terms from
\frac{\partial^{2}L_{T}(\theta,\tau)}{\partial\tau_{k}\partial\theta_{j}}
)
.

Under Assumptions
1
and
2
, there exists a closed ball
\overline{B_{L}}
centered at
(\theta_{0},\tau(\theta_{0}))
with strictly positive radius s.t., for all
(k,j)\in[\![1,m]\!]^{2}
,

(i)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1,}\theta)}\right]<\infty
;

(ii)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{\ell}}\psi_{k}(X_{1},\theta)|\right]<\infty
;

(iii)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi_{k}(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{\ell}}|\right]<\infty
;

(iv)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{\ell}}|\right]<\infty
;

(v)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi_{k}(X_{1},\theta)|\right]<\infty
;

(vi)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}|\right]<\infty
;

(vii)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi_{k}(X_{1},\theta)\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}|\right]<\infty
;

(viii)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{j}}|\right]<\infty
;

(ix)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial^{2}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{j}\partial\theta^{\prime}}|\right]<\infty
;

(x)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi_{k}(X_{1},\theta)\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{j}}|\right]<\infty
;

(xi)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi_{k}(X_{1},\theta)\frac{\partial^{2}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{j}\partial\theta^{\prime}}|\right]<\infty
;

(xii)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta^{\prime}}\frac{\partial\psi_{k}(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{j}}|\right]<\infty
;

(xiii)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}|\right]<\infty
;

(xiv)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi_{k}(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}|\right]<\infty
;

(xv)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{j}}\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}|\right]<\infty
;

(xvi)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{j}}\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}|\right]<\infty
;

(xvii)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi_{k}(X_{1},\theta)\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{j}}\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}|\right]<\infty
;

(xviii)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\psi_{k}(X_{1},\theta)\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{j}}\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}|\right]<\infty
; and

(xix)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi_{k}(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{j}}\psi(X_{1},\theta)\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}|\right]<\infty
.

Proof.

The proofs are similar to the ones of Lemma
18
(p.
18
): We only use more often the inequality that states that the norm of a component of a vector is smaller than the norm of the vector (e.g.,
|\psi_{k}(X_{1},\theta)|\leqslant\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{m}\psi_{l}(X_{1},\theta)^{2}}=|\psi(X_{1},\theta)|
). Thus, we only provide proof sketches.

(i)
See Lemma
18
i p.
18
.

(ii)
For
\overline{B_{L}}
of sufficiently small radius, for all
(k,j)\in[\![1,m]\!]^{2}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\tau^{\prime}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{j}}\psi_{k}(X_{1},\theta)|\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle(\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\tau|)\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{j}}|\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\psi_{k}(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}|\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\leqslant}}

\displaystyle(\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\tau|)\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\mathrm{e}^{\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}\frac{\partial\psi(X_{1},\theta)}{\partial\theta_{j}}|^{2}\right]}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\psi_{k}(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}|^{2}\right]}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\leqslant}}

\displaystyle(\sup_{(\theta,\tau)\in\overline{B_{L}}}|\tau|)\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\theta\in\mathcal{N}}\sup_{\tau\in\mathbf{T}(\theta)}\mathrm{e}^{2\tau^{\prime}\psi(X_{1},\theta)}b(X_{1})^{2}\right]}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\theta\in\mathbf{\Theta}^{\epsilon}}|\psi(X_{1},\theta)^{\prime}|^{2}\right]}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{<}}\infty,

where the last inequality follows from Assumption
2
(b), and Lemma
9
i (p.
9
), under Assumption
1
(a)(b)(g).

(iii)
For
\overline{B_{L}}
of sufficiently small radius, for all

,

, where the last inequality follows from Assumption

(b).

.
